## Marine Transportation Security Regulations {#marine-transportation-security-regulations .Title-of-Act}

[SOR]{.abbr title="Statutory Orders and Regulations"}/2004-144
:::

[MARINE TRANSPORTATION SECURITY ACT](/eng/acts/M-0.8)

Registration 2004-05-21

Marine Transportation Security Regulations

P.C. 2004-726 2004-05-21
::::

:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
Her Excellency the Governor General in Council, on the recommendation of
the Minister of Transport, pursuant to section 5 of the [Marine
Transportation Security Act](/eng/acts/M-0.8)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#footnotea){#footnotea-ID0EDBAA}, hereby makes the
annexed [Marine Transportation Security
Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2004-144).

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#footnotea-ID0EDBAA){#footnotea}S.C. 1994, c. 40
  :::
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-709134 .Part}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2014-162, s. 1(F)

\]
:::

- **[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in these Regulations.

  [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Marine Transportation
      Security Act](/eng/acts/M-0.8). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[approved training course]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[approved training course]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a course
      that has been approved under section 803 and that is given by a
      recognized institution. ([cours de formation
      approuvé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[breach of security]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[breach of security]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      SOR/2014-162, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[CDC facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[CDC facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a marine facility where
      any of the following operations are carried out:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture or storage of certain dangerous
        cargoes intended to be transferred to a vessel to which Part 2
        applies;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the storage of certain dangerous cargoes
        transferred from a vessel to which Part 2 applies; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the transfer of certain dangerous cargoes to or
        from a vessel to which Part 2 applies. ([installation
        CCD]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[CDC residue]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[CDC residue]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of CDCs having the UN number UN1942,
        UN2067, UN2426 or UN3375 that are carried in a large means of
        containment and are intended for sale, residue that remains on
        board a vessel after the CDCs are unloaded, that does not exceed
        450 kg in total quantity, and each of whose accumulations has a
        volume that does not exceed 0.05 m^3^; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of liquids and liquefied gases that
        are carried in a large means of containment, residue that
        remains in the cargo unloading system after the liquids and
        liquefied gases are unloaded and that is not accessible using
        normal transfer procedures, with the exception of the residue of
        liquefied gases having the UN number UN1005, UN1017, UN1035,
        UN1040, UN1062, UN1079, UN1086 or UN1971. ([résidu de
        CCD]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[certain dangerous cargoes]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[certain dangerous cargoes]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      SOR/2014-162, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[certain dangerous cargoes]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[certain dangerous
      cargoes]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}*or*[[CDCs]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
       means the dangerous goods that are set out in Schedule 4 and that
      are listed in Schedule 1 to the [Transportation of Dangerous Goods
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286) or in Part 3 of the
      IMDG Code. ([certaines cargaisons dangereuses]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"}*ou*[CCD]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[container]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[container]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a structure for
      transporting commodities on trucks, railcars or vessels that meets
      the requirements set out in the [Safe Containers Convention
      Act](/eng/acts/S-1). ([conteneur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[container terminal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[container terminal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any marine
      facility that accepts containers for transport. ([terminal pour
      conteneurs]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[contracting government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[contracting government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
      government of a state that is a signatory to SOLAS. ([gouvernement
      contractant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[cruise ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[cruise ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel to which Part 2
      applies and that has sleeping facilities for over 100 persons who
      are not crew members, but does not include a ferry. ([navire de
      croisière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[cruise ship terminal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[cruise ship terminal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a marine
      facility that interfaces with cruise ships. ([terminal pour
      navires de croisière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[dangerous goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[dangerous goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means dangerous goods as
      defined in section 2 of the [Transportation of Dangerous Goods
      Act, 1992](/eng/acts/T-19.01). ([marchandises
      dangereuses]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[declaration of security]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[declaration of security]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement
      between a vessel and a marine facility or another vessel that is
      required by section 228 or 315. ([déclaration de
      sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[ferry]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ferry]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is used
      exclusively for the carriage of passengers, vehicles, or
      passengers in vehicles and that operates on a fixed schedule
      between two or more points. ([traversier]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[ferry facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ferry facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a marine facility
      that interfaces with ferries to which Part 2
      applies. ([installation pour traversiers]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[fishing vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[fishing vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned by
      subsection 1(1) of the [Marine Personnel
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2007-115). ([bâtiment de
      pêche]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[government vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[government vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel, other
      than one operated for a commercial purpose,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that is owned by and is in the service of Her
        Majesty in right of Canada or that is in the exclusive
        possession of Her Majesty in that right; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is owned by and is in the service of a
        foreign government or that is in the exclusive possession of a
        foreign government. ([bâtiment d'État]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[IMDG Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[IMDG Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the International Maritime
      Dangerous Goods Code, published by the International Maritime
      Organization, as amended from time to time. ([Code
      IMDG]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[interface]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[interface]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the interaction that
      occurs between a vessel and a marine facility, or between a vessel
      and another vessel, throughout the time that the vessel is
      alongside the marine facility or throughout the vessel-to-vessel
      activity, and includes the activities affected by the movement of
      persons and their goods, by the movement of cargo or by the
      provision of services to and from the
      vessel. ([interface]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[international voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[international voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a voyage

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} undertaken between a marine facility in Canada
        and a place outside Canada, or between places outside Canada, by
        a vessel that is entitled to fly the Canadian flag; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} undertaken in Canadian waters by a vessel that
        is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state. ([voyage
        international]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[ISPS Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ISPS Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the International Ship and
      Port Facility Security Code, as incorporated into SOLAS. ([Code
      ISPS]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[key]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[key]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device, including a card, that
      is designed to allow entry to a restricted area and is issued to
      an individual by an operator of a marine facility or vessel or
      port administration. ([clé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[large means of containment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[large means of containment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
      assigned by section 1.4 of the [Transportation of Dangerous Goods
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286). ([grand
      contenant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[marine security document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[marine security document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
      certificate, document or letter issued by the Minister under these
      Regulations. ([document de sûreté maritime]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[MARSEC level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[MARSEC level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the level of security
      requirements that is set by the Minister to reflect the threat
      environment for vessels, marine facilities and ports. ([niveau
      MARSEC]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[MARSEC level 1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[MARSEC level 1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the level for which
      minimum security requirements are required to be implemented in
      accordance with the approved security plan or approved security
      procedures. ([niveau MARSEC 1]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[MARSEC level 2]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[MARSEC level 2]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the level for which
      additional security requirements are required to be implemented as
      a result of heightened risk in accordance with the approved
      security plan or approved security procedures. ([niveau MARSEC
      2]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[MARSEC level 3]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[MARSEC level 3]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the level for which
      additional security requirements are required to be implemented as
      a result of a security threat that is probable or imminent,
      whether or not the specific target is identified, in accordance
      with the approved security plan or approved security
      procedures. ([niveau MARSEC 3]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[non-SOLAS ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[non-SOLAS ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is not
      a SOLAS ship, that is engaged on an international voyage, and that

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is more than 100 gross tonnage but is not a
        towing vessel;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is carrying more than 12 passengers; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is a towing vessel engaged in towing astern or
        alongside, or pushing ahead, a barge that is carrying certain
        dangerous cargoes. ([navire non ressortissant à
        SOLAS]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[occasional-use marine facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[occasional-use marine facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
      marine facility that, in a calendar year, has 10 or fewer
      interfaces with vessels to which Part 2 applies. ([installation
      maritime à usage occasionnel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a vessel,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the actual owner, if the vessel is not
        registered, or the registered owner, if the vessel is
        registered;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person having a beneficial interest in the
        vessel, including an interest arising under contract and any
        other equitable interest, other than an interest by way of a
        mortgage;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a lessee or charterer of the vessel who is
        responsible for its navigation; or

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is a barge referred to in
        paragraph (c) of the definition [[non-SOLAS
        ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, the master or any other person who
        has command or charge of the vessel that is towing or pushing
        the barge. ([exploitant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[passenger]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[passenger]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned by
      section 2 of the [Canada Shipping Act,
      2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15). ([passager]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[passenger facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[passenger facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a marine facility
      that interfaces with passenger vessels to which Part 2
      applies. ([installation pour passagers]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[pleasure craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[pleasure craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned by
      section 2 of the [Canada Shipping Act,
      2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15). ([embarcation de
      plaisance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[port]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[port]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a port as defined in section 5 of the [Canada
        Marine Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7);

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a public port designated under regulations made
        under section 65 of the [Canada Marine Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7) in
        which a marine facility that interfaces with a vessel to which
        Part 2 applies is situated; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a group of marine facilities, in close
        proximity to each other, whose operators agree with each other
        to be subject to sections 362 to 375. ([port]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[port administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[port administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operator of a marine facility that is a
        port authority incorporated under section 8 of the [Canada
        Marine Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7);

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an employee of the Department of Transport who
        is designated by the Minister as the security officer for a port
        as defined in paragraph (c) of the definition
        [[port]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the absence of a port administration
        described in paragraph (a) or (b), the operator of a marine
        facility who is identified in an agreement referred to in
        paragraph (c) of the definition [[port]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} to
        act as the port administration of the port. ([organisme
        portuaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[recognized institution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[recognized institution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a training
      institution that is designated as a recognized institution by the
      Minister under section 803.1 and that administers, in accordance
      with the requirements and established practices of the domestic
      and international marine industry, approved training courses for
      the purpose of providing the training necessary to obtain a
      certificate of proficiency issued under Part 8. ([établissement
      reconnu]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[restricted area pass]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[restricted area pass]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a document
      issued by an operator of a marine facility or vessel or port
      administration that entitles the holder, during a specified
      period, to have access to specific restricted areas in vessels,
      marine facilities or ports. ([laissez-passer de zone
      réglementée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[security breach]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[security breach]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a violation of these
      Regulations, of a security measure formulated under subsection
      7(1) of the Act, of a security rule formulated under subsection
      10(2) or (3) of the Act, or of a security procedure set out in an
      approved security plan or approved under subsection 360(1), that
      does not result in a security incident. ([infraction à la
      sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[security clearance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[security clearance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      SOR/2014-162, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[security incident]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[security incident]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an event during
      which the security of a vessel, marine facility or port is
      compromised. ([incident de sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[security threat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[security threat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any suspicious act
      or circumstance that could compromise the security of a vessel,
      marine facility, port or interface. ([menace contre la
      sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[SOLAS]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[SOLAS]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the International Convention
      for the Safety of Life at Sea, 1974, as amended from time to
      time. ([SOLAS]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[SOLAS ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[SOLAS ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is 500 gross tonnage or more or is carrying
        more than 12 passengers; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is engaged on an international voyage other
        than a voyage solely on the Great Lakes and the St. Lawrence
        River as far seaward as a straight line drawn from
        Cap-des-Rosiers to West Point, Anticosti Island, and from
        Anticosti Island to the north shore of the St. Lawrence River
        along the meridian of longitude 63° W. ([navire ressortissant à
        SOLAS]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[STCW Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[STCW Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Seafarers' Training,
      Certification and Watchkeeping Code, published by the
      International Maritime Organization, as amended from time to
      time. ([Code STCW]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[STCW Convention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[STCW Convention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the International
      Convention on Standards of Training, Certification and
      Watchkeeping for Seafarers, published by the International
      Maritime Organization, as amended from time to time. ([Convention
      STCW]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[towing vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[towing vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that tows
      another vessel astern or alongside or that pushes another vessel
      ahead. ([bâtiment remorqueur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[transportation security clearance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[transportation security clearance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
      security clearance granted by the Minister under section
      509. ([habilitation de sécurité en matière de
      transport]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[vessel personnel without security responsibilities]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[vessel personnel without security
      responsibilities]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means crew members, other
      than the vessel security officer or the company security officer,
      who do not have security responsibilities under the vessel's
      security plan. ([personnel du bâtiment n'ayant pas des
      responsabilités en matière de sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[vessel personnel with security responsibilities]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[vessel personnel with security
      responsibilities]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means crew members, other
      than the vessel security officer or the company security officer,
      who have security responsibilities under the vessel's security
      plan. ([personnel du bâtiment ayant des responsabilités en matière
      de sûreté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of interpreting the ISPS Code,
  [[company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} shall be read to mean
  [[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of the definition [[international
  voyage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in subsection (1), a vessel that is
  entitled to fly the Canadian flag

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} begins its international voyage at the beginning of
    its last interface with a marine facility in Canada before it
    engages on the voyage; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ends its international voyage at the end of its
    first interface with a marine facility in Canada when it returns
    from the voyage.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of the definition [[certain
  dangerous
  cargoes]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}*or*[[CDCs]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in
  subsection (1), a reference to a UN number in Schedule 4 is a
  reference to the goods that are listed in Schedule 1 to the
  [Transportation of Dangerous Goods
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286) or in Part 3 of the IMDG
  Code.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 1
- SOR/2007-275, s. 1
- SOR/2014-162, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-1-20080101.html "Link to previous version of section 1"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
nothing in these Regulations derogates from any right otherwise provided
to a representative of seafarers' welfare and labour organizations to
access a vessel or a marine facility, unless there is an immediate
threat to the safety and security of the vessel or marine facility.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 3
:::

**\[3 to 10 reserved\]**

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-709225 .Part}

### [MARSEC Level, Official Languages and Prescribed Grounds]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709226 .Subheading}

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** The operator of a vessel
to which Part 2 applies and the operator of a marine facility or a port
administration to which Part 3 applies shall maintain MARSEC level 1 at
all times unless a higher MARSEC level is required by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 2
- SOR/2014-162, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11-20061102.html "Link to previous version of section 11"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** The operator of a vessel
that is entitled to fly the Canadian flag and to which Part 2 applies,
and the operator of a marine facility to which Part 3 applies, other
than the operator of a marine facility that is referred to in paragraph
(a) of the definition [[port administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in
subsection 1(1) or the security officer referred to in paragraph (b) of
that definition, shall ensure that, where there is a significant demand
from at least 5 per cent of the travelling public for services in either
official language within the meaning of the [Official Languages
(Communications with and Services to the Public)
Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-92-48),

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} authorized screening is carried out by means that
  effectively enable communication with members of the public in the
  official language of their choice; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} printed or pre-recorded material is provided in both
  official languages if the material is used in respect of authorized
  screening.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 47(1) of the Act, the prescribed grounds are those that exist
when a decision is made in consideration of the possibility of an
immediate risk to the security of marine transportation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-270, s. 1
:::

### [Suspension or Cancellation of Marine Security Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709238 .Subheading}

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall suspend or cancel a marine security document issued to
  the operator of a vessel or marine facility, or to a port
  administration, if the Minister has reasonable grounds to believe that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the document was obtained by any fraudulent or
    improper means or by a misrepresentation of any material fact;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the document was altered, rendered illegible or
    lost;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the operator or port administration

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is not in compliance with its approved security
      plan or, in the case of an occasional-use marine facility, its
      security procedures approved under subsection 360(1), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} conducts its activities in a way that
      constitutes, or is likely to constitute, a threat to marine
      transportation security; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the requirements for issuance of the document are
    no longer met.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall notify the operator or the port
  administration of the suspension or cancellation by notice served in
  accordance with section 701. The notice shall include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the grounds for the suspension or cancellation and
    the date on which the suspension or cancellation takes effect; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the address at which, and the date --- being 30
    days after the day on which the notice is served --- on or before
    which, the operator or port administration may file a request to the
    Tribunal for a review of the suspension or cancellation.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The suspension or cancellation takes effect

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} no later than 30 days after the day on which the
    notice is served, if the ground for the suspension or cancellation
    is one of the grounds set out in paragraph (1)(a) or (b),
    subparagraph (1)(c)(i) or paragraph (1)(d); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at the time the notice is served, if the ground for
    the suspension or cancellation is the ground set out in subparagraph
    (1)(c)(ii).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a marine security document is suspended or
  cancelled, the operator or port administration to which it was issued
  shall return it immediately to the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 6
:::

### [Reviews and Appeals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709256 .Subheading}

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** The Tribunal has
jurisdiction in respect of reviews and appeals under sections 16 to 19.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 6
:::

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of a vessel or marine facility, or a port administration, may
  file with the Tribunal a written request for a review of a suspension
  or cancellation referred to in subsection 14(1) on or before the date
  specified in the notice of suspension or cancellation or within any
  further time that the Tribunal allows on application.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A request for a review does not operate as a stay of
  the suspension or cancellation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 6
:::

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  receipt of a request filed under section 16, the Tribunal shall
  appoint a time and place for the review and shall notify in writing
  the Minister and the operator of the vessel or marine facility, or the
  port administration, of the time and place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The assigned member of the Tribunal shall confirm the
  suspension or cancellation or refer the matter back to the Minister
  for reconsideration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 6
:::

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within
  30 days after the day on which a determination is made under
  subsection 17(2), the operator of the vessel or marine facility, or
  the port administration, may appeal the determination to the Tribunal.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party who does not appear at a review hearing is
  not entitled to appeal a determination unless they establish that
  there was sufficient reason to justify their absence.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The appeal panel of the Tribunal assigned to hear the
  appeal shall dismiss it or refer the matter back to the Minister for
  reconsideration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 6
:::

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** If a matter is referred
back to the Minister for reconsideration under subsection 17(2) or
18(3), the suspension or cancellation remains in effect until the
reconsideration is concluded. However, the member who made the
determination or the appeal panel, after considering any representations
made by the parties, may grant a stay of the suspension or cancellation
until the reconsideration is concluded if the member or panel determines
that granting a stay would not constitute a threat to marine
transportation security.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 6
:::

**\[20 to 199 reserved\]**

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Vessels]{.HTitleText1} {#h-709280 .Part}

**[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2014-162, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-200-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 200"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709282 .Subheading}

- **[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part applies to vessels in Canada, and Canadian ships outside Canada,
  that are SOLAS ships or non-SOLAS ships.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Part does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a pleasure craft, a fishing vessel, a vessel that
    is owned or operated by a government and used only on government
    non-commercial service, a vessel without a crew that is in dry dock,
    dismantled or laid-up, or a vessel that is subject to a security
    arrangement concluded between the Government of Canada and a
    contracting government in application of Regulation 11 of Chapter
    XI-2 of SOLAS; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a vessel that is entitled to fly the Canadian flag
    and that is engaged on a voyage between marine facilities in Canada,
    except when the vessel has an interface with a SOLAS ship or
    non-SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly the flag of a foreign state
    that is not subject to a security arrangement referred to in
    paragraph (a).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections 211(6) and 213(2),
  [[Administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the Canadian flag, the Minister; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the flag of a foreign state, the contracting government of that
    state.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-201-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 201"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Ship Security Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709293 .Subheading}

- **[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall issue an International Ship Security Certificate, in
  English or French, in respect of a Canadian ship that is a SOLAS ship
  if the requirements of paragraph 204(1)(b) and sections 205, 207 to
  218, 220, 222 to 225 and 260 are met.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall issue a Canadian Vessel Security
  Certificate, in English or French, in respect of a vessel that is
  entitled to fly the Canadian flag and is a non-SOLAS ship if the
  requirements of paragraph 204(1)(b) and sections 205, 207 to 218, 220
  and 260 are met.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall issue an interim International
  Ship Security Certificate or Canadian Vessel Security Certificate, as
  the case may be, in English or French, if the vessel security plan is
  approved but an inspection under section 23 of the Act has not yet
  been carried out to ensure compliance with the requirements referred
  to in subsection (1) or (2), as the case may be.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A certificate issued under subsection (1) or (2) is
  valid while the vessel security plan is valid.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} An interim certificate issued under subsection (3) is
  valid until the expiry of six months after the day on which it is
  issued or until a certificate is issued under subsection (1) or (2),
  whichever comes first.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 202"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Compliance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709302 .Subheading}

- **[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly the Canadian flag
  shall ensure that the requirements of sections 204 to 268 are met.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a non-SOLAS ship that is entitled to
  fly the Canadian flag shall ensure that the requirements of sections
  204 to 221 and 228 to 268 are met.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
  the flag of a foreign state shall ensure that the requirements of the
  following are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 204, paragraph 205(1)(e), subparagraph
    205(1)(f)(ii), paragraphs 205(1)(h) and (i), subsections 206(1) to
    (4), paragraph 206(5)(b), subsection 211(1), paragraphs 211(3)(b),
    212(h) to (h.2) and 213(1)(b), subsection 213(2), paragraph
    214(1)(b) and sections 219, 221, 228 and 260 to 268; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 7 and 9 to 13, other than sections 7.8,
    7.9 and 9.2, of Part A of the ISPS Code.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a non-SOLAS ship that is entitled to
  fly the flag of a foreign state shall ensure that the requirements of
  the following are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 204, paragraph 205(1)(e), section 206,
    subsections 211(1) and (2), 213(3) and 214(2) and sections 219, 221,
    228 and 260 to 268; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 7 and 9 to 13, other than sections 7.8,
    7.9, 9.2 and 9.4.16, of Part A of the ISPS Code.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} In the event of any conflict between the requirements
  of the ISPS Code and the provisions of these Regulations, the
  provisions of these Regulations prevail to the extent of the conflict.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-275, s. 2
- SOR/2014-162, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203-20080101.html "Link to previous version of section 203"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Documents to Be Carried on Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709315 .Subheading}

- **[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel shall carry on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the original of the certificate described in
    subsection (2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a vessel security plan approved

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel entitled to fly the
      Canadian flag, by the Minister, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel entitled to fly the flag
      of a foreign state, by a contracting government or by a security
      organization referred to in section 9.2 of Part A of the ISPS
      Code;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a continuous synopsis record issued by a
    contracting government, if the vessel is a SOLAS ship;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a record of the last 10 calls at marine facilities;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the last 10 declarations of security; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is a SOLAS ship, the original of
    every certificate of proficiency required by this Part for the
    vessel security officer and for vessel personnel with or without
    security responsibilities.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The certificate referred to in paragraph (1)(a) is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the Canadian flag, a certificate issued under subsection 202(1) or
    (3);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a non-SOLAS ship that is entitled to
    fly the Canadian flag, a certificate issued under subsection 202(2)
    or (3);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the flag of a foreign state, an international ship security
    certificate, or an interim international ship security certificate,
    issued by the contracting government of that state; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a non-SOLAS ship that is entitled to
    fly the flag of a foreign state, a ship security compliance
    document, or an interim ship security compliance document, issued or
    approved by the contracting government of that state.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security officer on a SOLAS ship and a
  member of the vessel personnel with or without security
  responsibilities on a SOLAS ship shall, if they hold a certificate of
  proficiency, ensure that their certificate is carried on board.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-275, s. 3
- SOR/2014-162, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-204-20080101.html "Link to previous version of section 204"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Operator of a Vessel]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709334 .Subheading}

- **[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of a vessel shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish an administrative and organizational
    structure for the security of the vessel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide every person who has responsibilities under
    this Part with the support needed to fulfil his or her
    responsibilities;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} designate in writing a company security officer and
    a vessel security officer, by name or by position, other than the
    position for which the designation is being made;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} implement and maintain the vessel security plan,
    including any corrective action referred to in paragraph 209(h),
    and, if applicable, the marine facility security plan or the port
    security plan;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} provide the master with the following information:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the names of the parties responsible for
      appointing shipboard personnel, such as vessel management
      companies, crewing agents, contractors and concessionaires,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the names of the parties responsible for
      deciding the employment of the vessel, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is under a charter-party, the
      names of contact persons for the charter-party;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship, ensure that the vessel
    security officer and vessel personnel with or without security
    responsibilities have the ability to maintain their effectiveness
    while performing their duties in accordance with the requirements
    set out

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is entitled to fly the
      Canadian flag, in the safe manning document issued under section
      202 of the [Marine Personnel
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2007-115), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a ship that is entitled to fly
      the flag of a foreign state, in the safe manning document issued
      by the contracting government of that state;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel that is entitled to fly the
    Canadian flag, ensure that a vessel security plan is established;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the vessel security officer and vessel
    personnel with or without security responsibilities meet the
    training requirements set out in sections A-VI/5 and A-VI/6 of the
    STCW Code; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} clearly identify each restricted area on the
    vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a SOLAS ship shall permanently mark
  the ship with the International Maritime Organization Ship
  Identification Number in accordance with Regulation 3 of Chapter XI-1
  of SOLAS.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 205"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Master]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709354 .Subheading}

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Part permits any
  person to constrain the master of a vessel from making or executing
  any decision that, in the professional judgment of the master, is
  necessary to maintain the safety and security of the vessel, including
  decisions

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to deny access to persons, other than operators and
    persons identified as authorized by a contracting government, or
    their goods;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to refuse to load cargo, including containers or
    other closed cargo transport units; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to coordinate, with marine facility operators or
    port security officers, shore leave for vessel personnel or crew
    change, as well as access through marine facilities of visitors to
    the vessel, including representatives of seafarers' welfare and
    labour organizations.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a conflict between any safety and security
  requirements applicable to the vessel arises during its operations,
  the master shall give precedence to requirements intended to maintain
  the safety of the vessel and, in such a case, shall use any temporary
  procedures that the master determines appropriate under the
  circumstances and, to the highest possible degree, satisfy the
  security requirements of the prevailing MARSEC level.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the master uses temporary procedures, the master
  shall, as soon as practicable, inform

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is in Canadian waters, a Marine
    Communications and Traffic Services Centre of the Canadian Coast
    Guard;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is a Canadian ship in the waters of a
    contracting government, the relevant maritime authority of that
    government and a Marine Communications and Traffic Services Centre
    of the Canadian Coast Guard; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is a Canadian ship in other waters, a
    Marine Communications and Traffic Services Centre of the Canadian
    Coast Guard.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The master shall provide the vessel security officer
  with the support necessary to carry out their duties on board the
  vessel.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The master shall ensure that the vessel security
  officer and vessel personnel with or without security responsibilities
  carry on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship entitled to fly the
    Canadian flag, their certificates of proficiency issued under Part
    8; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship entitled to fly the
    flag of a foreign state, their certificates of proficiency issued by
    the contracting government of that state.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Company Security Officer]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709371 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709372 .Subheading}

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  company security officer may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} act in that capacity for more than one vessel if
    they are able to fulfil their responsibilities for each vessel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have other responsibilities within the vessel
    operator's organization if they are able to fulfil the
    responsibilities of company security officer; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} delegate tasks required by this Part.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A company security officer remains responsible for
  the performance of the tasks they delegate.

#### [Qualifications]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709379 .Subheading}

**[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208 .sectionLabel}** A company security
officer shall have, by training or job experience, knowledge and
competence that is relevant to the industry in which the vessel operates
and is in the following areas:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the administrative and organizational structure for
  the security of the vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operations and operating conditions of vessels,
  ports and marine facilities;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the security procedures of vessels, ports, port
  administrations and marine facilities, including the meanings and the
  requirements of the different MARSEC levels;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} emergency preparedness and response and contingency
  planning;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} security equipment and systems and their operational
  limitations;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} methods of conducting audits and inspections;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} access control and monitoring techniques;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} methods of conducting on site surveys and vessel
  security assessments;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} methods of conducting physical searches and
  non-intrusive inspections;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} conducting and assessing security drills and
  exercises, including exercises with marine facilities;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} techniques for security training and education;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} relevant international conventions, codes, standards
  and recommendations;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} relevant legislation, regulations and security
  measures, rules and procedures;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} the responsibilities and functions of municipal,
  provincial and federal law enforcement agencies;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} methods for handling security-sensitive information
  and security-related communications;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} current security threats and patterns;

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} the recognition and detection of weapons, explosives
  and incendiaries and other dangerous substances and devices;

- [(r)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
  behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;
  and

- [(s)]{.lawlabel} techniques that might be used to violate security
  procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment or systems.

#### [Responsibilities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709400 .Subheading}

**[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** A company security
officer shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide the vessel security officer with information
  regarding security threats and other information relevant to the
  vessel's security;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the vessel security assessment and any
  amendments are submitted to the Minister for approval;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the vessel security plan and any
  amendments are submitted to the Minister for approval;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the vessel security plan, as amended from
  time to time, is implemented;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} ensure that security drills and exercises are
  conducted;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the vessel's security activities are
  audited;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} as soon as practicable after a deficiency in the
  vessel security plan is identified, amend the plan to correct the
  deficiency;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} ensure that corrective action is implemented as soon
  as practicable to correct any deficiency referred to in paragraph (g)
  until the vessel security plan is amended;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} ensure security awareness and vigilance on board the
  vessel;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} ensure that vessel personnel receive security
  training as required by this Part;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the security orientation referred to in
  section 214 is provided;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} ensure that there is effective communication and
  cooperation between the vessel and marine facilities or other vessels
  with which the vessel interfaces;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} ensure that there is consistency between the security
  requirements and the safety requirements;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} if a vessel security plan is prepared for more than
  one vessel, ensure that the plan takes into account the
  characteristics specific to each vessel; and

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship, ensure that the safe
  manning document is applied in the development of security procedures.

### [Vessel Security Officer]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709417 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709418 .Subheading}

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  vessel security officer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be the master or a member of the crew;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may also act as the vessel security officer for a
    vessel without a crew if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they are able to fulfil their responsibilities
      for each vessel, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} each vessel's security plan lists the other
      vessels for which they are responsible;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} may have other responsibilities within the vessel
    operator's organization if they are able to fulfil the
    responsibilities of vessel security officer;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} may delegate tasks required by this Part; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a towing vessel, shall coordinate
    its security procedures with those of any vessels that it is towing
    or pushing.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A vessel security officer remains responsible for the
  performance of the tasks they delegate.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-275, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-210-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 210"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Qualifications]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709431 .Subheading}

- **[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  vessel security officer shall have knowledge of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the layout of the vessel; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel security plan and its requirements.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A non-SOLAS ship's vessel security officer shall
  have, by training or job experience, knowledge and competence that are
  relevant to the industry in which the vessel operates and are in the
  following areas:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} those set out for a company security officer in
    paragraphs 208(a) to (m) and (o) to (s);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} crowd management and control techniques; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the operation, testing, calibration and maintenance
    of security equipment and systems.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), a SOLAS ship's vessel
  security officer shall hold

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the Canadian flag, a certificate of proficiency as a ship security
    officer issued under Part 8; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the flag of a foreign state, a certificate of proficiency as a ship
    security officer issued by the contracting government of that state.

- [(4) and (5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-162, s.
  14\]]{.Repealed}

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} In circumstances of exceptional necessity, when a
  person who holds a certificate of proficiency as a ship security
  officer is temporarily unavailable to serve as vessel security officer
  on a SOLAS ship, the Administration may permit a member of the
  vessel's personnel who has responsibilities respecting the security of
  the vessel and an understanding of the vessel security plan to serve
  as vessel security officer on the vessel until it arrives at the next
  port of call or for a period not exceeding 30 days. That member shall
  serve as the vessel security officer as permitted by the
  Administration, and the vessel's master shall, as soon as possible,
  inform the relevant maritime authorities of the next ports of call of
  the arrangements in place.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-162, s. 14\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-275, s. 5
- SOR/2014-162, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-211-20080101.html "Link to previous version of section 211"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Responsibilities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709448 .Subheading}

**[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** A vessel security
officer shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} while the vessel is in operation, conduct inspections
  of the vessel at the frequency specified in the vessel security plan
  to ensure compliance with the requirements of this Part;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} implement the vessel security plan, as amended from
  time to time;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} conduct audits of the vessel security plan as
  required by this Part;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} coordinate the implementation of the vessel security
  plan with the company security officer and, if applicable, with the
  port security officer and the marine facility security officer;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} as soon as practicable after a deficiency in the
  vessel security plan is identified, report it to the company security
  officer and implement the action necessary that is required to correct
  the deficiency until the plan is amended;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} propose amendments to the vessel security plan to the
  company security officer to correct any deficiencies;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} ensure security awareness and vigilance on board the
  vessel, including awareness of changes in the MARSEC level and other
  circumstances that might affect work conditions on board;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} ensure that appropriate security training or
  orientation is provided to the vessel's personnel in accordance with
  this Part;

- [(h.1)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all persons employed or engaged on
  board a vessel, including contractors, receive security orientation
  that is appropriate to their duties and the time they have spent on
  board the vessel, in order to be able to

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} report a security incident,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} know the procedures to follow when there is a
    security threat, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} take part in security-related emergency and
    contingency procedures;

- [(h.2)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all vessel personnel with security
  responsibilities receive, before carrying out any of those
  responsibilities, security orientation that is appropriate to their
  duties;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} report security threats and security incidents to the
  master, the company security officer, the appropriate law enforcement
  agencies, the Minister and, if applicable, the port administration, as
  soon as possible after they occur so that an investigation can be
  conducted;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} report security breaches to the Minister and, if
  applicable, the port administration, as soon as possible after they
  occur;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} ensure that security equipment is operated, tested,
  calibrated and maintained in compliance with the vessel security plan;
  and

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} conduct security drills and exercises.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-212-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 212"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Vessel Personnel with Security Responsibilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709469 .Subheading}

- **[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  member of the vessel personnel with security responsibilities on a
  SOLAS ship shall hold

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the Canadian flag, a certificate of proficiency as a member of the
    vessel personnel with security responsibilities issued under Part 8;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the flag of a foreign state, a certificate of proficiency as a
    member of the ship personnel with security responsibilities issued
    by the contracting government of that state.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In circumstances of exceptional necessity, if a
  member of the vessel personnel with security responsibilities is
  temporarily unavailable to fulfil his or her security responsibilities
  on a SOLAS ship, the Administration may permit a member of the vessel
  personnel without security responsibilities but with a knowledge of
  the vessel security plan to fulfil those responsibilities until the
  ship arrives at the next port of call or for a period not exceeding 30
  days.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A member of the vessel personnel with security
  responsibilities on a non-SOLAS ship shall have, by training or job
  experience, knowledge and competence that are relevant to the industry
  in which the ship operates in any of the following areas that relate
  to the member's responsibilities:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} current security threats and patterns;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the recognition and detection of weapons,
    explosives and incendiaries, and other dangerous substances and
    devices;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
    behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} techniques that could possibly be used to violate
    security procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment
    or systems;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} crowd management and control techniques;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} security-related communications;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} emergency preparedness and response and contingency
    planning;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the operation, testing, calibration and maintenance
    of security equipment and systems;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} inspection and monitoring techniques;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} methods of performing physical searches of persons
    and goods, including personal effects, baggage, ships' stores and
    cargo;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the relevant provisions of the vessel security
    plan; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the meanings and the requirements of the different
    MARSEC levels.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-213-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 213"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Vessel Personnel without Security Responsibilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709490 .Subheading}

- **[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  member of the vessel personnel without security responsibilities on a
  SOLAS ship shall hold

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the Canadian flag, a certificate of proficiency as a member of the
    vessel personnel without security responsibilities issued under Part
    8; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship that is entitled to fly
    the flag of a foreign state, a certificate of proficiency as a
    member of the ship personnel without security responsibilities
    issued by the contracting government of that state.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A member of the vessel personnel without security
  responsibilities on a non-SOLAS ship, and a contractor engaged on the
  ship, shall receive security orientation that is appropriate to their
  duties and the amount of time that they have spent on board the ship.
  The security orientation shall address the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the meanings of the different MARSEC levels, the
    procedures at each level and the emergency procedures and
    contingency plans;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the recognition and detection of weapons,
    explosives and incendiaries, and other dangerous substances and
    devices;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
    behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} techniques that could possibly be used to violate
    security procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment
    or systems.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 17
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-214-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 214"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Security Drills and Exercises]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709502 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709503 .Subheading}

**[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** Security drills and
exercises shall test the proficiency of vessel personnel in carrying out
their assigned security responsibilities at all MARSEC levels and the
effective implementation of the vessel security plan and shall enable
the vessel security officer to identify any related security
deficiencies that need to be rectified.

#### [Security Drills]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709505 .Subheading}

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  security drill may be conducted with other drills, if appropriate, but
  shall be conducted at least once every three months, except in the
  case of a vessel that is entitled to fly the Canadian flag and that is
  not engaged on an international voyage or is not in service at the
  time that a drill is to be conducted, in which case the drill shall be
  conducted within one week after the beginning of an international
  voyage or the return to service.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Security drills shall test individual elements of the
  vessel security plan, including responses to security threats,
  security breaches and security incidents, and shall take into account,
  in respect of the vessel, the types of operations, personnel changes
  and other relevant circumstances.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-162, s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, at any given time, more than 25 per cent of the
  vessel's permanent crew have not participated in a security drill on
  board the vessel within the previous three months, a security drill
  shall be conducted within one week.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A documented response to a security incident that was
  recorded under paragraph 218(1)(c) is considered equivalent to a
  security drill.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, ss. 18, 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 216"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Security Exercises]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709514 .Subheading}

- **[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Security exercises

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall test the requirements of the vessel security
    plan that are relevant to the objectives of each exercise and shall
    involve the active participation of personnel on board the vessel as
    appropriate to the exercise;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may include the participation of governmental
    authorities or personnel from marine facilities, port
    administrations or other vessels who have security responsibilities,
    depending on the scope and the nature of the exercises;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} may be performed with respect to only the vessel or
    as part of a cooperative program to test the vessel security plan of
    another vessel or a marine facility or port security plan; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} shall test, at a minimum, elements of coordination,
    resource availability, response, and communication and notification
    procedures.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Security exercises shall be conducted at least once
  every calendar year with no more than 18 months between them.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Security exercises may be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} full-scale;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a tabletop simulation or seminar;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} combined with other appropriate exercises; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a combination of two or more of the elements set
    out in paragraphs (a) to (c).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The implementation of security procedures at MARSEC
  level 2 or MARSEC level 3 is considered equivalent to a security
  exercise.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 19
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-217-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 217"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Record Keeping]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709530 .Subheading}

- **[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  vessel security officer shall keep the following records:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} details of security training, including the date,
    duration and description and the names of the participants;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} details of security drills and exercises, including
    the date and description, the names of the participants and any best
    practices or lessons learned that might improve the vessel security
    plan;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} details of security threats, security breaches and
    security incidents, including the date, time, location and
    description, the response to them and the person to whom they were
    reported;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} records of changes in the MARSEC level, including
    the date, the time that notification was received and the time of
    compliance with the requirements of the new level;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} a record of the date on which, and the time at
    which, the vessel becomes subject to this Part and of the date on
    which, and the time at which, it ceases to be subject to this Part;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} records of maintenance, calibration and testing of
    equipment used for security, including the date and time of the
    activity and the equipment involved;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the last 10 declarations of security and of each
    continuing declaration of security for at least 90 days after the
    end of its effective period;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a SOLAS ship, a continuous synopsis
    record issued by the Minister;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a record of the last 10 calls at marine facilities;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a record of each annual audit of the vessel
    security plan that includes, for each annual audit, a letter from
    the company security officer or the vessel security officer that
    certifies the day on which the audit was completed;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the vessel security assessment and
    details of each periodic review of the vessel security assessment,
    including the date on which it was conducted and the findings of the
    review;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the approved vessel security plan and details of
    each periodic review of the vessel security plan, including the date
    on which it was conducted, the findings of the review and any
    amendments to the plan that are recommended;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} details of each amendment to the vessel security
    plan, including the date of its approval and of its implementation;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} a list, by name or position, of the vessel
    personnel who have security responsibilities;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} an up-to-date list containing the names of
    screening officers; and

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} records of inspections, including the date on which
    they are conducted.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If records respecting equipment that is not used
  exclusively for security are kept separately from records respecting
  equipment that is used exclusively for security, the vessel security
  officer shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} document, in written or electronic form, their
    existence, location and the name or position of the person
    responsible for keeping them; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the records are accessible.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security officer shall ensure that the
  records set out in subsection (1) are kept for at least two years
  after they are made and shall make them available to the Minister on
  request, but shall ensure that the copy of the vessel security
  assessment and the approved vessel security plan are kept for at least
  two years after the expiry of the plan.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security officer shall ensure that the
  records are protected from unauthorized access or disclosure.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security officer shall ensure that any
  records kept in electronic format are protected from deletion,
  destruction and revision.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} No person shall disclose security information
  contained in the records, unless the disclosure is for the purpose of
  complying with these Regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 3
- SOR/2006-270, s. 2
- SOR/2014-162, ss. 20, 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-218-20071102.html "Link to previous version of section 218"){.PITLink}
:::

### [MARSEC Level Coordination and Implementation of Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709558 .Subheading}

- **[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of a vessel shall, before the vessel interfaces with a marine
  facility, ensure that all procedures that are established in the
  vessel security plan for compliance with the MARSEC level in effect
  for that marine facility are implemented.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} When notified of an increase in the MARSEC level, the
  master of a vessel shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the MARSEC level in effect at a marine facility
    with which the vessel is about to interface is raised to a level
    higher than the level in effect on the vessel, ensure that the
    vessel complies, without undue delay before interfacing with the
    facility, with all the procedures established in the vessel security
    plan for compliance with that higher MARSEC level;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is in Canadian waters, notify a
    Marine Communications and Traffic Services Centre of the Canadian
    Coast Guard if all the procedures established in the vessel security
    plan for compliance with that higher MARSEC level have not been
    implemented;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is a Canadian ship in the waters of a
    contracting government, notify the relevant maritime authority of
    that government;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is a Canadian ship in other waters,
    notify a Marine Communications and Traffic Services Centre of the
    Canadian Coast Guard; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} brief all vessel personnel on identified security
    threats, emphasize reporting procedures and stress the need for
    increased vigilance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If there has been a change in the MARSEC level from
  that reported in the pre-arrival information required by section 221,
  the master shall immediately notify a Marine Communications and
  Traffic Services Centre of the Canadian Coast Guard.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-219-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 219"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Communications]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709570 .Subheading}

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  vessel security officer shall have the means to notify all vessel
  personnel of changes in security conditions on board the vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vessel shall have communication systems and
  procedures that allow for effective communications

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} between personnel with security responsibilities
    with respect to the vessel and marine facilities, ports and other
    vessels interfacing with the vessel; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the Minister and local law enforcement
    agencies.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The communication systems shall have a backup to
  ensure internal and external communications.

### [Pre-arrival Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709577 .Subheading}

- **[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel shall ensure that the vessel does not enter
  Canadian waters unless the master reports pre-arrival information at
  the following time to the Minister in accordance with the instructions
  set out in the most recent edition of the Canadian Coast Guard Radio
  Aids to Marine Navigation:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the duration of the segment of the voyage before
    entering Canadian waters is less than 24 hours, as soon as
    practicable before entering Canadian waters but no later than the
    time of departure from the last port of call;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the duration of the segment of the voyage before
    entering Canadian waters is less than 96 hours but more than 24
    hours, at least 24 hours before entering Canadian waters; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at least 96 hours before entering Canadian waters.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel shall ensure that the
  following pre-arrival information in respect of the vessel is reported
  under subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its name;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its country of registry;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name of its registered owner;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name of its operator;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of its classification society;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} its international radio call sign;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the number of its International Ship Security
    Certificate or interim International Ship Security Certificate, of
    its Canadian Vessel Security Certificate or interim Canadian Vessel
    Security Certificate, or of its ship security compliance document or
    interim ship security compliance document;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} its International Maritime Organization ship
    identification number, if it is a SOLAS ship;

  - [(h.1)]{.lawlabel} as applicable, the International Maritime
    Organization Unique Company Identification Number of its company and
    the International Maritime Organization Unique Registered Owner
    Identification Number of its owner, as defined in Regulation 3-1 of
    Chapter XI-1 of SOLAS;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of issuance, date of expiry and name of
    the issuing body of its International Ship Security Certificate or
    interim International Ship Security Certificate, of its Canadian
    Vessel Security Certificate or interim Canadian Vessel Security
    Certificate, or of its ship security compliance document or interim
    ship security compliance document;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} confirmation that the vessel has an approved vessel
    security plan;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the current MARSEC level;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} a statement of when its last 10 declarations of
    security were completed;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} details of any security breach, security incident
    or security threat involving the vessel during the last ten calls at
    marine facilities and during the time spent at sea between those
    calls;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-162, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} details of any deficiencies in its security
    equipment and systems, including the communication systems, and the
    way in which the master of the vessel intends to correct them;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the name of its agent and their
    24-hour telephone and facsimile numbers;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the name of its charterer;

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the information is reported and
    the latitude and longitude of the vessel at the time that it is
    reported;

  - [(s)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's course and speed;

  - [(t)]{.lawlabel} its first port of call in Canada, with the
    estimated time of arrival at that port of call, and, if applicable,
    its final destination, with the estimated time of arrival at that
    destination;

  - [(u)]{.lawlabel} the name of a contact person at the marine facility
    that it will visit and their 24-hour telephone and facsimile
    numbers;

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the following information in respect of its last 10
    marine facilities visited:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the receiving facility,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility visited,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the city and country,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the date and time of arrival, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the date and time of departure;

  - [(w)]{.lawlabel} a general description of the cargo, including the
    cargo amount;

  - [(x)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the presence and description of any
    dangerous substances or devices on board; and

  - [(y)]{.lawlabel} the following contact information:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the name of the master,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an email address, if applicable, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a satellite or cellular telephone number, if
      applicable.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If there is any change in the pre-arrival information
  reported under subsection (1), the master of a vessel shall ensure

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the change is reported to the Minister in
    accordance with the instructions set out in the most recent edition
    of the Canadian Coast Guard publication entitled Radio Aids to
    Marine Navigation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the vessel does not enter Canadian waters
    unless the change has been reported to the Minister; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a change that has occurred after the
    vessel has entered Canadian waters, that the change is reported to
    the Minister before the vessel's first interface with a marine
    facility in Canada.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply to vessels operating
  solely on the Great Lakes or to the portions of a vessel's voyage on
  the Great Lakes after pre-arrival information has been given prior to
  its entrance into the St. Lawrence Seaway.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 4(F)
- SOR/2006-270, s. 3
- SOR/2014-162, s. 22
:::
