[Previous
Version](section-221-20071102.html "Link to previous version of section 221"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Vessel Security Alert System]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709625 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2006-269, s. 5(F)

\]
:::

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section, a vessel is constructed on the earliest
  of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which its keel is laid,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which construction identifiable with a
    specific vessel begins, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the assembly of the vessel reaches
    the lesser of 50 tonnes and 1 per cent of the estimated mass of all
    structural material of the vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a vessel shall ensure that the vessel
  is equipped with a vessel security alert system

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not later than the day of the first survey of its
    radio installation after July 1, 2004 if the vessel was constructed
    before July 1, 2004 and is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a passenger vessel, including a passenger
      high-speed craft, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an oil tanker, a chemical tanker, a gas carrier,
      a bulk carrier or a cargo high-speed craft that is 500 tons gross
      tonnage or more;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} not later than the day of the first survey of its
    radio installation after July 1, 2006 if the vessel was constructed
    before July 1, 2004 and is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a cargo vessel, other than one referred to in
      subparagraph (a)(ii), that is 500 tons gross tonnage or more, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a mobile offshore drilling unit; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} before it is operated if the vessel was constructed
    on or after July 1, 2004.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A vessel's radio installation may be used as the
  vessel security alert system if it meets the requirements of Chapter
  IV of SOLAS and those of sections 223 to 225.

<!-- -->

- **[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  vessel security alert system shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be capable of being activated from the navigation
    bridge and at least one other location;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} comply with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} International Maritime Organization Resolution
      MSC.136(76), annex 7, Performance Standards for a Ship Security
      Alert System, as amended from time to time, if the system was
      installed before July 1, 2004,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} International Maritime Organization Resolution
      MSC.147(77), annex 5, Performance Standards for a Ship Security
      Alert System, as amended from time to time, if the system is
      installed on or after July 1, 2004; or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} another performance standard that provides the
      same level of security as in subparagraph (i) or (ii); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be equipped with activation points designed to
    prevent its inadvertent initiation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of interpreting the annexes referred
  to in paragraph (1)(b), "should" shall be read to mean "shall".

**[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** The vessel security
alert system, when activated,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall, if the security of the vessel is under threat
  or has been compromised, initiate and transmit a vessel-to-shore
  security alert to the nearest Canadian maritime rescue coordination
  centre identifying the vessel and its position and indicating that the
  security of the vessel is under threat or has been compromised;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall not send a security alert to another vessel;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall not raise an alarm on board the vessel; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} shall continue the security alert until it is
  deactivated or reset.

<!-- -->

- **[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  radio system used for a vessel security alert system shall comply with
  relevant international standards.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the vessel security alert system is powered from
  the vessel's main source of electrical power, it shall also be
  possible to operate the system from another source of power.

**[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** If a Canadian maritime
rescue coordination centre notifies the Minister that it has received a
vessel security alert, the Minister shall immediately notify the
contracting governments in the vicinity of which the vessel is operating
and, in the case of a Canadian ship, its operator.

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** If a Canadian maritime
rescue coordination centre notifies the Minister that it has received a
vessel security alert from a vessel that is entitled to fly the flag of
a foreign state, the Minister shall immediately notify the contracting
government of that vessel and, if appropriate, of the countries in the
vicinity of which the vessel is operating.

### [Declaration of Security]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709661 .Subheading}

- **[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  declaration of security shall be completed before an interface starts
  between a vessel and a marine facility or another vessel if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are operating at different MARSEC levels;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one of them does not have a security plan approved
    by a contracting government or by a security organization referred
    to in section 9.2 of Part A of the ISPS Code;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the interface involves a cruise ship, a vessel
    carrying certain dangerous cargoes or the loading or transfer of
    certain dangerous cargoes; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the security officer of either of them identifies
    security concerns about the interface.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A new declaration of security is required if there is
  a change in the MARSEC level.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The declaration of security shall provide a means for
  ensuring that all shared security concerns are fully addressed
  throughout the interface and shall contain the information set out in
  the form in Appendix 1 of Part B of the ISPS Code, with the terms
  "ship", "port facility" and "security measures" read as "vessel",
  "marine facility" and "security procedures", respectively.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The declaration of security shall be in English or
  French and be signed by the vessel security officer and the marine
  facility security officer or the vessel security officers, as the case
  may be.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A vessel security officer or a marine facility
  security officer may authorize in writing a person who has security
  responsibilities on the vessel or marine facility and appropriate
  training to complete and sign the declaration of security on their
  behalf.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} At MARSEC level 1 and MARSEC level 2, a continuing
  declaration of security may be used for multiple interfaces between a
  vessel and a marine facility or another vessel if the effective period
  of the declaration does not exceed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 90 days at MARSEC level 1; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 30 days at MARSEC level 2.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} If a declaration of security is required under
  subsection (1) between a vessel and the operator of a lock in the St.
  Lawrence Seaway, it shall be completed on its entry into the first
  lock and remain in effect until the vessel exits the St. Lawrence
  Seaway at the St. Lambert Lock or the Welland Canal at Port Colborne.

### [Vessel Security Assessment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709676 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709677 .Subheading}

**[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** The persons who conduct
a vessel security assessment shall have, collectively, the competence to
evaluate the security of the vessel, including knowledge that is
relevant to the industry in which the vessel operates, in the following
areas:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} current security threats and patterns;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the detection and recognition of weapons, explosives
  and incendiaries and other dangerous substances and devices;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
  behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} techniques that might be used to violate security
  procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment or systems;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} methods used to cause a security incident;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the effects of dangerous substances and devices on
  vessels and their equipment;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} vessel security requirements;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} vessel-to-vessel and vessel-to-marine facility
  interface business practices;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} emergency preparedness and response and contingency
  planning;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} physical security requirements;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} radio and telecommunications systems, including
  computer systems and networks;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} marine engineering; and

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} vessel and marine facility operations.

#### [Security Assessment Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709692 .Subheading}

**[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** The company security
officer shall ensure that the following security assessment information
is provided to persons who conduct the on-site survey and vessel
security assessment:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the general layout of the vessel, including the
  location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} actual and potential points of access to the vessel
    and their function,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} areas that should have restricted access,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} essential maintenance equipment,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} stowage and cargo spaces, including storage areas
    for essential maintenance equipment, ships' stores, cargo and
    unaccompanied baggage, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} ships' stores;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} security threat assessments, including the purpose
  and methodology of the assessment, for the area in which the vessel
  operates or at which passengers embark or disembark and the types of
  cargo being carried by the vessel;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a copy of any previous security assessment prepared
  for the vessel;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a list of the emergency and stand-by equipment
  available to maintain essential services;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} changes in the tide that might have an impact on the
  vulnerability or security of the vessel;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the number of vessel personnel, the security duties
  of persons with security responsibilities and existing security
  training requirements;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a list of existing security and safety equipment for
  the protection of personnel, visitors and passengers;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} details of escape and evacuation routes and assembly
  stations that have to be maintained to ensure the orderly and safe
  emergency evacuation of the vessel;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} copies of existing agreements with persons or
  organizations that provide security services; and

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} details of security procedures in effect, including
  inspection and access control procedures, identification systems,
  surveillance and monitoring equipment, personnel identification
  documents, communication, alarm, lighting, access control and other
  security systems.

#### [On-site Survey]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709709 .Subheading}

**[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** The company security
officer shall ensure that an on-site survey of the vessel is conducted
that examines and evaluates protective measures, procedures and
operations in effect on board the vessel to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure the performance of all vessel security duties;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} control access to the vessel through the use of
  identification systems or otherwise;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} control the embarkation of vessel personnel and other
  persons and their goods, including personal effects and baggage,
  whether accompanied or unaccompanied;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} supervise the handling of cargo and the delivery of
  ships' stores;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} monitor restricted areas and other areas that have
  restricted access to ensure that only authorized persons have access;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} monitor the deck areas and areas adjacent to the
  vessel; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} ensure the ready availability of security
  communication systems, information and equipment.

#### [Analysis and Recommendations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709718 .Subheading}

**[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** The persons who conduct
a vessel security assessment shall take into account the security
assessment information, the on-site survey and the requirements of this
Part and provide recommendations for security procedures that shall be
established in the vessel security plan, including recommendations
respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} restricted areas;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} response procedures for fire or other emergency
  conditions;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} security supervision of all persons on board;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the frequency and effectiveness of security patrols;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} access control systems, including identification
  systems;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} security communication systems and procedures;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} security doors, barriers and lighting;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} security and surveillance equipment and systems;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} potential security threats and the following types of
  security incidents:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} damage to, or destruction of, the vessel or an
    interfacing marine facility or vessel by explosive devices, arson,
    sabotage or vandalism,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} tampering with the essential equipment or systems,
    stores or cargo of the vessel,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the unauthorized access to or use of the vessel,
    including the presence of stowaways,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the smuggling on board of weapons, explosives,
    incendiaries or other dangerous substances or devices, including
    weapons of mass destruction,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the use of the vessel or its equipment as a weapon
    or as a means to cause damage or destruction,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the hijacking or seizure of the vessel or persons
    on board, and

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} attacks on the vessel while at berth, at anchor
    or at sea; and

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the evaluation of the potential of each identified
  point of access, including open decks, that could be used by
  individuals who might try to breach security, whether or not those
  individuals legitimately have access to the vessel.

#### [Content]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709737 .Subheading}

- **[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  vessel security assessment shall be written in English or French and
  shall contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a summary of how the on-site survey was conducted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} details of existing security procedures and
    operations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of each vulnerability found during
    the assessment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of security procedures that should be
    used to address each vulnerability;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a list of the key vessel operations that are
    important to protect;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} conclusions as to the likelihood of possible
    security threats to key vessel operations; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a list of identified weaknesses, including human
    factors, in the infrastructure, policies and procedures relating to
    the vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A vessel security assessment shall address the
  following elements in respect of the vessel:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its physical security;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its structural integrity;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} personnel protection systems;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} security procedures;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} its radio and telecommunication systems, including
    computer systems and networks; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any other element on board the vessel that might,
    if damaged or used illicitly, pose a risk to people, property or
    operations on board the vessel or at a marine facility.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security assessment shall consider the
  security of all persons and the activities, services, operations,
  capacities and goods that are important to protect, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the capacity to maintain safe navigation and
    emergency response;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cargo, particularly dangerous goods or substances;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ships' stores;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's security communication and
    surveillance systems, if any; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other security systems on the vessel.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security assessment shall take into
  account all possible vulnerabilities, including those resulting from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any conflict between safety and security
    requirements;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any conflict between duties on board and security
    assignments;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the impact of watchkeeping duties and fatigue on
    vessel personnel alertness and performance;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} security training deficiencies;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} deficiencies in security equipment and systems,
    including communication systems; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the fact that the vessel was not subject to this
    Part before the start of an international voyage.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 23
:::

### [Vessel Security Plan]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709769 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709770 .Subheading}

- **[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  vessel security plan shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} state the name of the vessel's operator;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the company security officer by name or,
    if they hold another position, by position, and provide 24-hour
    contact information;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of vessel entitled to fly the Canadian
    flag, be written in English or French;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be based on the vessel security assessment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} address each vulnerability identified in the vessel
    security assessment;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} establish that the vessel's master has the
    overriding authority and responsibility to make decisions with
    respect to the security of the vessel and to request the assistance
    of the operator or contracting government when necessary; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} identify the locations of the vessel security alert
    system activation points.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security plan shall address the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} procedures designed to prevent weapons, explosives,
    incendiaries, dangerous substances and devices that are intended for
    use against persons, vessels or marine facilities and whose carriage
    is not authorized from being taken on board the vessel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} procedures for the prevention of unauthorized
    access to the vessel that include the security procedures set out in
    sections 236 to 239 and, if applicable, sections 260 and 264;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} procedures for the establishment of restricted
    areas as provided for in section 240;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} procedures for preventing unauthorized access to
    restricted areas that include the security procedures set out in
    sections 241 to 243;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} procedures for cargo handling and for ships' stores
    and bunkers that include the security procedures set out in sections
    244 to 251;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} procedures for monitoring the vessel, the
    restricted areas on board the vessel and the area surrounding the
    vessel that include the security procedures set out in sections 252
    to 255;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} procedures for responding to security threats,
    security breaches and security incidents, including provisions for
    maintaining critical operations of the vessel or for vessel and
    marine facility interfacing, that include the security procedures
    set out in section 256;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} procedures for responding to any security
    instructions a contracting government may give at MARSEC level 3 in
    respect of a specific security threat;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} other security procedures for each MARSEC level;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} procedures for evacuation in case of security
    threats, security breaches or security incidents;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} duties of shipboard personnel assigned security
    responsibilities and of other shipboard personnel on security
    related matters;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} procedures for auditing the security activities;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} procedures for training, drills and exercises
    associated with the plan;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} procedures for interfacing with marine facilities
    and other vessels at all MARSEC levels;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} procedures for declarations of security;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} procedures for the periodic review of the plan and
    for updating it;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} procedures for reporting security incidents;

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} procedures to ensure the inspection, testing,
    calibration and maintenance of any security equipment on board;

  - [(s)]{.lawlabel} the frequency for testing or calibration of any
    security equipment on board;

  - [(t)]{.lawlabel} the frequency of inspections of the vessel;

  - [(u)]{.lawlabel} procedures, instructions and guidance on the use of
    the vessel security alert system, including its testing, activation,
    deactivation and resetting and how to limit false alerts;

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} procedures to facilitate shore leave for vessel
    personnel or crew change;

  - [(w)]{.lawlabel} procedures for overcoming any vulnerability
    resulting from the fact referred to in paragraph 233(4)(f); and

  - [(x)]{.lawlabel} procedures to be followed when the vessel becomes
    subject to this Part and when it ceases to be subject to this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, ss. 24, 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-234-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 234"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Format]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709807 .Subheading}

**[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** The vessel security
plan shall include the following individual sections and, if the plan
does not list the sections in the following order, it shall contain an
index that identifies the location of the sections:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} organizational structure for the security of the
  vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} personnel training;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} drills and exercises;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} records and documentation;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} response to a change in the MARSEC level;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} procedures for interfacing with marine facilities and
  other vessels;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} declarations of security;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} communications;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} security systems and equipment maintenance;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} security procedures for access control;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} security procedures for restricted areas;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} security procedures for handling cargo;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} security procedures for delivery of ships' stores and
  bunkers;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} security procedures for monitoring;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} procedures for security threats, security breaches
  and security incidents;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} audits and vessel security plan amendments; and

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} vessel security assessment summary.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 101(E)
:::

:::

### [Security Procedures for Access Control]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709828 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709829 .Subheading}

- **[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  all MARSEC levels, security procedures shall be established in the
  vessel security plan, to the extent that they are appropriate to the
  vessel's operations, to control access to the vessel (in particular,
  access to ladders, access gangways, access ramps, access doors, side
  scuttles, windows and ports, hatches, mooring lines, anchor chains,
  cranes and hoisting gear) and to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} deter the unauthorized introduction of weapons,
    explosives and incendiaries and other dangerous substances and
    devices;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} carry out authorized screening; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} secure weapons, explosives and incendiaries and
    other dangerous substances and devices that are authorized to be on
    board.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security plan shall ensure that the
  following are established at each MARSEC level:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the type of restriction or prohibition to be
    applied and the means of enforcing it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the means of identification required to allow
    persons to board the vessel and remain on the vessel without having
    their identity questioned.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security plan shall ensure that an
  identification system for verifying the identification of vessel
  personnel or other persons seeking to board the vessel is established
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} allows the identification of authorized persons,
    temporarily or permanently, at each MARSEC level;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is coordinated, when practicable, with
    identification systems at marine facilities used by the vessel; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is updated regularly.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security plan shall indicate the frequency
  of application of any security procedure for access control,
  particularly if the security procedure is applied on a random or
  occasional basis.

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709843 .Subheading}

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 1,
security procedures for access control shall be established in the
vessel security plan, to the extent that they are appropriate to the
vessel's operations, including

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the authorized screening of persons and goods at a
  frequency specified in the plan;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} verifications of the identification of any person
  seeking to board the vessel and examining at least one of the
  following:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} joining instructions,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a passenger ticket,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a boarding pass,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} work orders or surveyor orders,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} governmental identification,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a restricted area pass, or

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} visitor badges issued in accordance with an
    identification system;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the denial or revocation of a person's authorization
  to be on board if the person is unable or unwilling, at the request of
  vessel personnel, to establish their identity or to account for their
  presence on board;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} reporting of any incident referred to in
  paragraph (c) to the company security officer, vessel security
  officer, marine facility security officer, port security officer, if
  applicable, and the appropriate law enforcement agencies;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} means to deter unauthorized access to the vessel;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the identification of access points that must have
  secured means of entry or be attended to deter unauthorized access;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the locking of, or the prevention in some other way
  of access to, unattended spaces that adjoin areas to which passengers
  and visitors have access;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the provision of a designated secure area on board
  or, in liaison with a marine facility, at a marine facility, for
  conducting the authorized screening of persons and goods;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the authorized screening of vessel personnel or their
  goods by other vessel personnel, if it is clearly required for
  security reasons;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the segregation of persons who and their goods that
  have been through authorized screening from other persons and their
  goods;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} the segregation of embarking passengers from
  disembarking passengers;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} the authorized screening before loading, at the
  frequency specified in the vessel security plan and in liaison with a
  marine facility, of a defined percentage of vehicles to be loaded
  aboard a passenger vessel;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} the authorized screening before loading, in liaison
  with a marine facility, of all unaccompanied vehicles to be loaded on
  a passenger vessel; and

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} a response to the presence of unauthorized persons on
  board, including the expulsion of unauthorized boarders.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709866 .Subheading}

**[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 2,
additional security procedures for access control shall be established
in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are appropriate to
the vessel's operations, including

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an increase in the frequency and detail of the
  authorized screening of persons and goods;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the authorized screening of all unaccompanied baggage
  by means of X-ray equipment;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the assignment of additional personnel to patrol deck
  areas during periods of reduced vessel operations to deter
  unauthorized access;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a reduction in the number of access points to the
  vessel;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the denial of access to visitors who do not have a
  verified destination;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the deterrence of waterside access to the vessel,
  which may include, in liaison with a marine facility or a port
  administration, providing boat patrols; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is at a marine facility, the
  establishment of a restricted area on the shoreside of the vessel, in
  close cooperation with the facility.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709875 .Subheading}

**[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 3,
additional security procedures for access control shall be established
in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are appropriate to
the vessel's operations, including

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the authorized screening of all persons and goods;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the performance of one or more of the following
  procedures on unaccompanied baggage:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more extensive authorized screening, such as
    X-raying it from two or more angles,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} preparing to restrict or suspend handling it, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} refusing to accept it on board;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} preparation for cooperating with response
  organizations and marine facilities;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the limitation of access to the vessel to a single,
  controlled access point;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the granting of access to only those responding to
  the security incident or security threat;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the suspension of embarkation or disembarkation
  activities;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the suspension of operations related to cargo;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the evacuation of the vessel;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the movement of the vessel; and

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the preparation for a full or partial search of the
  vessel.

### [Security Procedures for Restricted Areas]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709890 .Subheading}

#### [Establishment of Restricted Areas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709891 .Subheading}

- **[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  all MARSEC levels, security procedures for restricted areas shall be
  established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are
  appropriate to the vessel's operations, in order to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevent or deter unauthorized access;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} protect the vessel, security areas within the
    vessel, security and surveillance equipment and systems and persons
    authorized to be on board; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} protect cargo and ships' stores from tampering.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Restricted areas shall be established for the
  following areas:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the navigation bridge, machinery spaces and other
    control stations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} spaces containing the central controls for security
    and surveillance equipment and systems and spaces that contain the
    central lighting system controls;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ventilation and air-conditioning systems and other
    similar spaces;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} spaces with access to potable water tanks, pumps or
    manifolds;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} spaces containing dangerous goods or hazardous
    substances;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} spaces containing cargo pumps and their controls;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} cargo spaces and spaces containing ships' stores;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} crew accommodations; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any other spaces or areas vital to the security of
    the vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel security plan shall contain procedures to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} identify vessel personnel who are authorized to
    have access;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} determine the persons other than vessel personnel
    who are authorized to have access;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} determine the conditions under which vessel
    personnel or other persons are authorized to have access;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} define the extent of any restricted area;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} establish the times when access restrictions apply;
    and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} post notices in accordance with section 21 of the
    Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 240"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709916 .Subheading}

**[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 1, the
following security procedures for restricted areas shall be established
in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are appropriate to
the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the locking or securing of access points;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the monitoring and use of surveillance equipment;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the use of guards or patrols; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the use of automatic intrusion detection devices to
  alert vessel personnel to unauthorized access by the activation of an
  audible or visual alarm at a location that is continuously attended or
  monitored.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709922 .Subheading}

**[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 2, the
following additional security procedures for restricted areas shall be
established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are
appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an increase in the frequency and intensity of
  monitoring and access controls on existing restricted areas;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of restricted areas adjacent to
  access points;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the continuous monitoring of each area, using
  surveillance equipment; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the assignment of additional personnel to guard or
  patrol each area.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709928 .Subheading}

**[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 3, the
following additional security procedures for restricted areas shall be
established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are
appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of additional restricted areas on
  the vessel in proximity to the location of a security incident or the
  presumed location of a security threat; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} searches of restricted areas as part of a security
  sweep of the vessel.

### [Security Procedures for Handling Cargo]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709932 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709933 .Subheading}

- **[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  all MARSEC levels, security procedures in relation to cargo handling
  shall be established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that
  they are appropriate to the vessel's operations, in order to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} deter and detect tampering;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent cargo that is not meant for carriage from
    being accepted and stored on board the vessel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} identify cargo that is approved for loading onto
    the vessel;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} include inventory control procedures at access
    points to the vessel; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} be able to inspect cargo for dangerous substances
    and devices at a frequency specified in the plan by one or more of
    the following means:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} visual examination,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} physical examination,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} detection devices such as scanners, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} canines.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The company security officer or vessel security
  officer may, with the agreement of the marine facility security
  officer and, if applicable, the port security officer, make
  arrangements with shippers or others responsible for the cargo with
  respect to off-site inspection, sealing, scheduling, supporting
  documentation and similar matters. The company security officer or
  vessel security officer, as the case may be, shall inform the marine
  facility security officer and, if applicable, the port security
  officer of any arrangement.

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709946 .Subheading}

**[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 1, the
following security procedures in relation to cargo handling shall be
established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are
appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the routine inspection, including for evidence of
  tampering, of cargo and cargo spaces before and during cargo handling,
  except where it is unsafe to do so;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} verification that cargo to be loaded matches the
  cargo documentation or that cargo markings or container numbers match
  the information provided with shipping documents;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} inspection before loading, in accordance with the
  frequency specified in the plan and in liaison with marine facilities,
  of vehicles to be loaded on board ro-ro vessels, car-carriers and
  passenger vessels; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} verification, in liaison with marine facilities, of
  seals or other means used to prevent tampering.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709952 .Subheading}

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 2, the
following additional security procedures in relation to cargo handling
shall be established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that
they are appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an increase in the frequency and detail of inspecting
  cargo and cargo spaces;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} intensified inspections to ensure that only the
  intended cargo, container or other cargo transport units are loaded;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} intensified inspections of vehicles to be loaded on
  board ro-ro vessels, car-carriers and passenger vessels;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} increases, in liaison with the marine facility, in
  the frequency and detail of inspections of seals or other means used
  to prevent tampering;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} increases in the frequency of the use of detection
  devices, such as scanners, or canines; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the coordination of enhanced security procedures with
  the shipper or other responsible party in accordance with an
  established agreement and procedures.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709960 .Subheading}

**[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 3, the
following additional security procedures in relation to cargo handling
shall be established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that
they are appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the suspension of loading or unloading of cargo;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} preparation for cooperation with response
  organizations and marine facilities; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a verification of the inventory and location of any
  hazardous substances carried on board.

### [Security Procedures for Delivery of Ships' Stores and Bunkers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709965 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709966 .Subheading}

- **[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  all MARSEC levels, security procedures for the delivery of ships'
  stores and bunkers shall be established in the vessel security plan,
  to the extent that they are appropriate to the vessel's operations, in
  order to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} inspect ships' stores for package integrity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent ships' stores from being accepted without
    inspection;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prevent tampering; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} prevent ships' stores and bunkers from being
    accepted without being ordered.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a vessel routinely uses a marine facility, the
  vessel security plan may establish standing procedures involving the
  vessel, its suppliers and the marine facility regarding notification
  and the timing of deliveries and their documentation.

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709974 .Subheading}

**[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 1, the
following security procedures with respect to the delivery of ships'
stores and bunkers shall be established in the vessel security plan, to
the extent that they are appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the inspection of ships' stores before they are
  accepted;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} verification that ships' stores and bunkers match the
  order before being brought on board or being bunkered; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the immediate securing of ships' stores following
  delivery.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709979 .Subheading}

**[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 2, the
following additional security procedures with respect to the delivery of
ships' stores shall be established in the vessel security plan, to the
extent that they are appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} intensified inspections of ships' stores during
  delivery; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the inspection of ships' stores before they are
  received on board.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709983 .Subheading}

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 3, the
following additional security procedures with respect to the delivery of
ships' stores and bunkers shall be established in the vessel security
plan, to the extent that they are appropriate to the vessel's
operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} more extensive inspections of ships' stores; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the restriction or suspension of handling of ships'
  stores and bunkers or refusal to accept them on board.

### [Security Procedures for Monitoring]{.HTitleText2} {#h-709987 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709988 .Subheading}

- **[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  all MARSEC levels, security procedures shall be established in the
  vessel security plan, to the extent that they are appropriate to the
  vessel's operations, to continuously monitor the vessel, the
  restricted areas on board the vessel and the area surrounding the
  vessel through a combination of lighting, watchkeepers, security
  guards, deck watches, automatic intrusion-detection devices,
  surveillance equipment and waterborne patrols.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following shall be considered when establishing
  the appropriate level and location of lighting:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} vessel personnel shall be able to detect activities
    on and around the vessel, on both the shoreside and the waterside;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the lighting shall facilitate personnel
    identification at access points;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the lighting may be provided through coordination
    with a marine facility or port administration; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} when lighting is necessary and the vessel is
    underway, the lighting shall be the maximum available that is
    consistent with safe navigation, taking into account the
    International Regulations for Preventing Collisions at Sea, 1972.

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-709996 .Subheading}

- **[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  MARSEC level 1, the following security procedures for monitoring the
  vessel shall be established in the vessel security plan, to the extent
  that they are appropriate to the vessel's operations:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} preparing to conduct emergency searches of the
    ship;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identifying and fixing any system failures or
    malfunctions of monitoring equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} continual monitoring of any automatic intrusion
    detection device that sets off an audible or visual alarm, or both;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} lighting the deck and vessel access points during
    the period between sunset and sunrise and periods of limited
    visibility sufficiently to allow the visual identification of
    persons seeking to board the vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} These security procedures may be coordinated with a
  marine facility or a port administration.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710004 .Subheading}

**[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 2, the
following additional security procedures for monitoring the vessel shall
be established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are
appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an increase in the frequency and detail of security
  patrols;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an increase in the coverage and intensity of
  lighting;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the use of or the increased use of security and
  surveillance equipment;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the assignment of additional personnel as security
  lookouts; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the coordination of monitoring with boat patrols, and
  with foot or vehicle patrols, if provided by a marine facility or a
  port administration.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710011 .Subheading}

**[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** At MARSEC level 3, the
following additional security procedures for monitoring the vessel shall
be established in the vessel security plan, to the extent that they are
appropriate to the vessel's operations:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} cooperation with response organizations, marine
  facilities and port administrations;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} switching on all lights on the vessel;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the illumination of the vicinity of the vessel;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the use of all surveillance equipment capable of
  recording activities on, or in the vicinity of, the vessel;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the maximization of the length of time the
  surveillance equipment can continue to record;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} preparations to conduct an underwater inspection of
  the hull; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} measures to deter underwater access to the hull,
  including slow revolutions of the vessel's propellers, if practicable.

#### [Security Procedures for Security Threats, Security Breaches and Security Incidents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710020 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2014-162, s. 101(E)

\]
:::

**[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256 .sectionLabel}** At all MARSEC levels,
security procedures shall be established in the vessel security plan, as
appropriate to the vessel's operations, for the vessel security officer
and persons who have security responsibilities with respect to the
vessel to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} respond to security threats, security breaches and
  security incidents and maintain critical vessel and vessel-to-facility
  interface operations, including by

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting entry into the affected area,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} denying access to the vessel, except to persons
    responding to the threat, breach or incident,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} implementing MARSEC level 3 security procedures
    throughout the vessel,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} stopping cargo-handling operations, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} notifying shoreside authorities or other vessels of
    the threat, breach or incident;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} evacuate the vessel in case of security threats,
  security breaches or security incidents, if the life of persons on
  board is threatened;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} report any security threats or security incidents
  involving the vessel to the Minister without undue delay;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} brief vessel personnel on potential security threats
  and the need for vigilance and their assistance in reporting
  suspicious persons, objects or activities; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} suspend non-critical operations in order to focus
  response on critical operations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, ss. 25, 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-256-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 256"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Submission and Approval]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710036 .Subheading}

- **[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  company security officer shall submit a vessel security plan to the
  Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a voyage that started before July 1,
    2004, no later than July 1, 2004;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a voyage that starts after June 30,
    2004 and before September 1, 2004, before the voyage starts; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a voyage that starts on or after
    September 1, 2004, at least 60 days before the voyage starts.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall approve a vessel security plan
  that meets the requirements of sections 234 and 235.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 259(5), a plan is valid for the
  period fixed by the Minister, not exceeding five years after the date
  on which it is approved by the Minister. In fixing the period, the
  Minister shall consider the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's operations and the industry in which
    it operates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's ports of call and usual routes;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the operator's security record;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's security record;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the complexity of the vessel security plan and the
    details of its procedures; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the findings of the vessel security assessment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 26
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-257-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 257"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Audits and Amendments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710052 .Subheading}

- **[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  company security officer shall ensure that an audit of the vessel
  security plan is performed annually.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the plan meets the requirements of sections 234
  and 235, the company security officer shall attach a letter to it
  certifying that it meets those requirements.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The company security officer shall ensure that an
  audit of the vessel security plan is performed if there is a new
  operator or if there have been modifications to the vessel, including
  to its physical structure, emergency response procedures or security
  procedures or operations. An audit of the plan as a result of
  modifications to the vessel may be limited to the provisions of the
  plan affected by the modifications.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Persons that perform audits of the security
  procedures established in the vessel security plan

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall have knowledge of the methods of conducting
    audits and inspections and control access and monitoring techniques;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall be independent of the security procedures
    being audited unless that is impracticable because of the size and
    nature of the vessel.

<!-- -->

- **[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an audit finds that the vessel security plan no longer meets the
  requirements of sections 234 and 235, the vessel security officer
  shall ensure that amendments are submitted to the company security
  officer within 30 days after the day on which the audit is completed.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a vessel may make amendments to a
  vessel security plan approved under section 257. The operator shall
  submit the amendments to the Minister 30 days, or any shorter period
  that the Minister allows, before the day on which they are to take
  effect.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a vessel shall submit amendments to
  the Minister within 60 days after the day on which the Minister
  informs the operator in writing that a vessel security plan approved
  under section 257 no longer meets the requirements of sections 234 and
  235.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall approve the amended plan if it
  meets the requirements of sections 234 and 235, unless approving the
  plan is not in the public interest and is likely to adversely affect
  marine transportation security.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If amendments are required under subsection (1)
  or (3) and they are not submitted or the amended plan is not approved,
  the plan ceases to be valid on the day on which the operator of the
  vessel receives notification that the plan is no longer valid.

### [Additional and Alternative Requirements in respect of Passenger Vessels and Ferries]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710066 .Subheading}

- **[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[passenger vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} does not
  include a cruise ship.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} At all MARSEC levels, after any period during which a
  passenger vessel or a ferry was unattended, its vessel security
  officer shall ensure that security sweeps are performed in order to
  confirm the absence of security threats, dangerous substances or
  devices before the vessel gets underway.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} At MARSEC level 1, instead of the identification
  verifications and authorized screening requirements referred to in
  paragraphs 237(a), (b) and (h), the vessel security plan of a
  passenger vessel or a ferry may establish

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} security procedures to search selected areas before
    passengers embark; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at least one of the following security procedures:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} conducting routine security patrols,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} using additional closed-circuit video cameras to
      monitor passenger areas, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} securing all non-passenger areas.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} At MARSEC level 2, the vessel security plan of a
  passenger vessel or a ferry shall establish security procedures to
  search selected areas before passengers embark and, if the plan is
  establishing procedures under subsection (3), to increase the patrols
  and monitoring referred to in paragraph (3)(b) and the security sweeps
  referred to in subsection (2).

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} At MARSEC level 3, if the vessel security plan of a
  passenger vessel or a ferry has established procedures under
  subsection (3), the plan shall establish random enhanced security
  patrols, which need not consist of vessel personnel.

### [Additional Requirements in respect of Cruise Ships]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710078 .Subheading}

#### [Weapons, Explosives and Incendiaries]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710079 .Subheading}

- **[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  no person shall possess or carry a weapon, explosives or incendiaries
  on board a cruise ship.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Members of a law enforcement agency in the
  performance of their duties may possess or carry a weapon on board a
  cruise ship.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Persons who are engaged in the handling or
  transportation of cash, negotiable instruments or other valuable goods
  and who require firearms to protect their lives in the course of that
  handling or transportation may possess or carry firearms on board a
  cruise ship that is docked when they are handling or transporting the
  goods on behalf of the cruise ship operator.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every cruise ship operator shall ensure that
  authorized screening of each person and their goods is carried out to
  ensure compliance with subsection (1).

**[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
embarking on or is on board a cruise ship shall not falsely declare

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} that they are carrying, or have in their goods, a
  weapon, explosives or incendiaries;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} that another person who is embarking on or is on
  board a cruise ship is carrying, or has in their goods, a weapon,
  explosives or incendiaries; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} that weapons, explosives or incendiaries have been
  placed on board a cruise ship.

#### [Authorized Screening]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710089 .Subheading}

- **[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  cruise ship operator shall remove from duty a screening officer who
  does not conduct an authorized screening in accordance with any
  security measures that may be formulated under section 7 of the Act
  and shall not allow the screening officer to conduct any authorized
  screening before the screening officer is retrained to meet the
  standards described in the measures.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A cruise ship operator shall maintain an up-to-date
  list containing the names of screening officers and shall provide the
  list to the Minister on request.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-263-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 263"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Security Procedures for Access Control]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710095 .Subheading}

**[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** At all MARSEC levels,
the cruise ship operator shall establish the following security
procedures to control access to the cruise ship:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} security patrols; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} searches of selected areas before passengers embark
  and before the vessel gets underway.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-270, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-264-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 264"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Security Briefs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710101 .Subheading}

**[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265 .sectionLabel}** When the MARSEC level
is raised to MARSEC level 3, the vessel security officer of a cruise
ship shall ensure that security briefs are provided to passengers about
the security threat that resulted in the MARSEC level being raised,
except where passengers might be put in danger.

### [Access]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710103 .Subheading}

#### [Prohibition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710104 .Subheading}

**[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** The operator of a SOLAS
ship or a non-SOLAS ship shall not allow a person to board the ship
unless the person is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a member of the crew of the ship;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a contractor or service provider engaged to perform
  work on the ship;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person referred to in any of paragraphs 267(1)(a)
  to (e); or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other person authorized by the operator or the
  master of the ship to come on board.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 27
:::

- **[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall enter or remain in a restricted area on a SOLAS ship or a
  non-SOLAS ship unless the person is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a member of the crew authorized to do so in
    accordance with the security procedures established in the vessel
    security plan;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person other than a member of the crew who is
    escorted by an individual authorized to do so in accordance with the
    security procedures established in the vessel security plan;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an inspector designated under subsection 22(1) of
    the Act who is on duty;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a member of any of the following groups who is on
    duty on board the ship or at a marine facility during an interface
    with the ship:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any police force in Canada,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Canadian Security Intelligence Service,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the Canadian Forces within the meaning of Part
      II of the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the Canada Border Services Agency; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a provider of emergency services who requires
    access to the restricted area for the protection and preservation of
    life or property.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall provide another person with access to
  a restricted area or assistance in entering a restricted area unless
  that other person is a person referred to in any of paragraphs (1)(a)
  to (e).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 27
:::

#### [Escorted Access]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710126 .Subheading}

- **[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who is being escorted in a restricted area shall remain with
  the escort while in the restricted area.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An escort shall remain with the person being
  escorted, or ensure that another person who is authorized to do so
  under the security plan acts as the escort, while the person being
  escorted is in the restricted area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 27
:::

**\[269 to 299 reserved\]**

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Marine Facilities]{.HTitleText1} {#h-710133 .Part}

**\[300 reserved\]**

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710135 .Subheading}

- **[[301]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-301 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[offshore facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a marine
  facility in a maritime zone of Canada as described in Part I of the
  [Oceans Act](/eng/acts/O-2.4) and includes a drilling unit and
  platform.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Part applies to marine facilities, other than
  offshore facilities, that interface with vessels to which Part 2
  applies.

### [Operator of a Marine Facility]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710139 .Subheading}

- **[[302]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-302 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of a marine facility shall ensure that the requirements of
  this Part are met.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of an occasional-use marine facility
  shall ensure that the requirements of sections 315 and 355 to 360.1
  are met.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-302-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 302"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[303]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-303 .sectionLabel}** The operator of a
marine facility shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish an administrative and organizational
  structure for security at the marine facility;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide every person who has responsibilities under
  this Part with the support needed to fulfil their responsibilities;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} designate in writing a marine facility security
  officer by name or by a position other than the position for which the
  designation is being made;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} operate the marine facility in compliance with the
  marine facility security plan and, if applicable, any corrective
  action referred to in paragraph 306(e) or a port security plan;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} submit the security assessment information to the
  Minister;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the implementation of security procedures
  is coordinated with the vessels with which the marine facility
  interfaces and, if the facility is in a port, with the port
  administration;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} if the marine facility is in a port, ensure the
  participation of the marine facility security officer in the port
  security committee;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} if the marine facility is in a port, ensure that the
  marine facility security officer develops a marine facility security
  plan with the port security officer and in consultation with
  representatives of appropriate law enforcement agencies, emergency
  response providers, employers and labour at the marine facility;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} coordinate, with the master of a vessel and, if the
  marine facility is in a port, the port security officer, shore leave
  for vessel personnel or crew change, as well as access through the
  marine facility of visitors to vessels, including representatives of
  seafarers' welfare and labour organizations;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} provide information respecting the security of the
  marine facility to those persons who need it to comply with these
  Regulations; and

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} clearly mark with signs each restricted area at the
  marine facility.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-303-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 303"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Marine Facility Security Officer]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710159 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710160 .Subheading}

- **[[304]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-304 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  marine facility security officer may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} act in that capacity for more than one marine
    facility if they are able to fulfil the responsibilities for each
    marine facility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have other responsibilities within the marine
    facility operator's organization if they are able to fulfil the
    responsibilities of marine facility security officer; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} delegate tasks required by this Part.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A marine facility security officer remains
  responsible for the performance of the tasks they delegate.

#### [Qualifications]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710167 .Subheading}

**[[305]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-305 .sectionLabel}** A marine facility
security officer shall have, by training or job experience, knowledge
and competence that is relevant to the industry in which the marine
facility operates and is in the following areas:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the administrative and organizational structure for
  security at the marine facility;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operations and operating conditions of the marine
  facility, vessels and if applicable, port;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the security procedures of the marine facility,
  vessels and, if applicable, port, including the meaning and the
  requirements of the different MARSEC levels;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} emergency preparedness and response and contingency
  planning;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} security equipment and systems and their operational
  limitations;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} methods of conducting audits and inspections;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} access control and monitoring techniques;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} methods of conducting on-site surveys and marine
  facility security assessments;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} methods of conducting physical searches and
  non-intrusive inspections;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} conducting and assessing security drills and
  exercises, including exercises with vessels;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} techniques for security training and education;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} relevant international conventions, codes, standards
  and recommendations;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} relevant legislation, regulations and security
  measures, rules and procedures;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} the responsibilities and functions of municipal,
  provincial and federal law enforcement agencies;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} methods of handling security-sensitive information
  and security-related communications;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} current security threats and patterns;

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} the recognition and detection of weapons, explosives
  and incendiaries and other dangerous substances and devices;

- [(r)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
  behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;

- [(s)]{.lawlabel} techniques that might be used to violate security
  procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment or systems;

- [(t)]{.lawlabel} crowd management and control techniques; and

- [(u)]{.lawlabel} the operation, testing, calibration and maintenance
  of security equipment and systems.

#### [Responsibilities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710190 .Subheading}

**[[306]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-306 .sectionLabel}** A marine facility
security officer shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct inspections of the marine facility at the
  rate specified in the marine facility security plan to ensure
  compliance with the requirements of this Part;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} submit a marine facility security plan and any
  amendment to the Minister for approval;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} implement and maintain the approved marine facility
  security plan, coordinating it when necessary with the security
  officer of interfacing vessels and the port security officer;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} conduct audits of the marine facility security plan
  in accordance with this Part;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} as soon as practicable after a deficiency in the
  marine facility security plan is identified, implement the corrective
  action that is required to correct the deficiency until the plan is
  amended;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} submit to the Minister amendments to the marine
  facility security plan to correct any deficiencies;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} implement the approved amendments in the marine
  facility security plan;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} ensure security awareness and vigilance at the marine
  facility, including awareness of changes in the MARSEC level and other
  circumstances that might affect work conditions at the marine
  facility;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} ensure that appropriate security training or
  orientation is provided to personnel at the marine facility in
  accordance with this Part;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} report security threats and security incidents to the
  appropriate law enforcement agencies, the Minister and, if applicable,
  the port administration, as soon as possible after they occur so that
  an investigation can be conducted;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} report security breaches to the Minister and, if
  applicable, the port administration, as soon as possible after they
  occur;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} ensure that there is effective communication and
  cooperation between the marine facility and the vessels with which it
  interfaces;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} ensure that security equipment is operated, tested,
  calibrated and maintained in compliance with the marine facility
  security plan;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} record and report the implementation of security
  procedures, after a change in the MARSEC level, to the Minister, to
  the operator of any vessel with which it is interfacing or about to
  interface and to the operator of any marine facility or port
  administration affected by the change;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} keep a copy of the marine facility security
  assessment and plan readily accessible; and

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} ensure that security drills and exercises are
  conducted.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-306-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 306"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Marine Facility Personnel with Security Responsibilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710210 .Subheading}

- **[[307]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-307
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Persons who have responsibilities
  respecting the security of a marine facility, other than the marine
  facility security officer, shall have, by training or job experience,
  knowledge that is relevant to the marine facility in the areas that
  relate to their responsibilities.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The areas of knowledge include the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} current security threats and patterns;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the recognition and detection of weapons,
    explosives and incendiaries and other dangerous substances and
    devices;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
    behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} techniques that might be used to violate security
    procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment or
    systems;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} crowd management and control techniques;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} security-related communications;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} emergency preparedness and response and contingency
    planning;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the operation, testing, calibration and maintenance
    of security equipment and systems;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} inspection and monitoring techniques;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} methods of performing physical searches of persons
    and goods, including personal effects, baggage, ships' stores and
    cargo;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the relevant provisions of the marine facility
    security plan; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the meaning and the requirements of the different
    MARSEC levels.

### [Marine Facility Personnel without Security Responsibilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710226 .Subheading}

**[[308]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-308 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
assigned to a marine facility and who does not have security
responsibilities shall receive security orientation in

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} basic security issues and communications;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the meaning and the requirements of the different
  MARSEC levels, the different procedures required of the person at each
  level and the emergency procedures and contingency plans;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the recognition and detection of weapons, explosives
  and incendiaries and other dangerous substances and devices;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
  behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;
  and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} techniques that might be used to violate security
  procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment or systems.

### [Security Drills and Exercises]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710233 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710234 .Subheading}

**[[309]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-309 .sectionLabel}** Security drills and
exercises shall test the proficiency of marine facility personnel in
carrying out their assigned security responsibilities at all MARSEC
levels and the effective implementation of the marine facility security
plan and shall enable the marine facility security officer to identify
any related security deficiencies that need to be corrected.

#### [Security Drills]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710236 .Subheading}

- **[[310]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-310 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  security drill is conducted

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least once every three months; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within one month after operations resume at a
    marine facility that has been out of service or inactive for more
    than three months.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Security drills shall test individual elements of the
  marine facility security plan, including the response to security
  threats, security breaches and security incidents, and shall take into
  account, in respect of the marine facility, the types of operations,
  personnel changes, the types of vessels interfacing with it and other
  relevant circumstances.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A documented response to a security incident that was
  recorded under paragraph 312(1)(c) is considered equivalent to a
  security drill.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, ss. 30, 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-310-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 310"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Security Exercises]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710245 .Subheading}

- **[[311]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-311
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Security exercises

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall test the requirements of the marine facility
    security plan that are relevant to the objectives of each exercise,
    and shall involve the active participation of marine facility
    personnel as appropriate to the exercise;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may involve the participation of governmental
    authorities or personnel from vessels, other marine facilities or
    port administrations who have security responsibilities, depending
    on the scope and the nature of the exercise;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} may be performed only with respect to the marine
    facility or as part of a cooperative program to test the security
    plan of another marine facility or the security plan of a vessel or
    a port; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} shall test, at a minimum, elements of coordination,
    resource availability, response, and communication and notification
    procedures.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Security exercises shall be conducted at least once
  every calendar year with no more than 18 months between them.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Exercises may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be full-scale or live;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be a tabletop simulation or seminar;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be combined with other appropriate exercises; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be a combination of two or more of the elements set
    out in paragraphs (a) to (c).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a marine facility is involved in the
  implementation of MARSEC level 2 or MARSEC level 3 following a
  security incident, the implementation of the MARSEC level is
  considered equivalent to a security exercise.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 31
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-311-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 311"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Record Keeping]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710261 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2006-270, s. 5

\]
:::

- **[[312]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-312 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  marine facility security officer shall keep

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} records of security training, including the date,
    the duration and description and the names of the participants;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} records of security drills and exercises, including
    the date and description, the names of the participants and any best
    practices or lessons learned that might improve the marine facility
    security plan;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} records of security threats, security breaches and
    security incidents, including the date, time, location and
    description, the response to them and the person to whom they were
    reported;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} records of changes in the MARSEC level, including
    the date, the time that notification was received and the time of
    compliance with the requirements of the new level;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} records of maintenance, calibration and testing of
    equipment used for security, including the date and time of the
    activity and the equipment involved;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} declarations of security in respect of the marine
    facility;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} internal audits and reviews of security activities;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} security assessment information;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility security assessment and each
    periodic review of the assessment, including the date on which the
    review was conducted and its findings;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility security plan and each periodic
    review of the plan, including the date on which the review was
    conducted, its findings and any recommended amendments to the plan;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} each amendment to the marine facility security
    plan, including the date of its approval and implementation;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} records of inspections and patrols;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} a list, by name or position, of the persons who
    have security responsibilities;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} an up-to-date list containing the names of
    screening officers; and

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} an up-to-date list, by name and position, of the
    holders of transportation security clearances, except in a case
    where a port administration keeps the list.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If records respecting equipment that is not used
