  exclusively for security are kept separately from records respecting
  equipment that is used exclusively for security, the marine facility
  security officer shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} document, in written or electronic form, their
    existence, location and the name or position of the person
    responsible for keeping them; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the records are accessible.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The marine facility security officer shall ensure
  that the records or documents listed in subsection (1) are kept for at
  least two years after the day on which they are made and shall make
  them available to the Minister on request, but shall ensure that the
  marine facility security plan, and the assessment on which it was
  based, are kept for at least two years after the day on which the plan
  expires.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The marine facility security officer shall ensure
  that the records are protected from unauthorized access or disclosure.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The marine facility security officer shall ensure
  that any records kept in electronic format are protected from
  deletion, destruction and revision.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} No person shall disclose security information
  contained in the records, unless the disclosure is for the purpose of
  complying with these Regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 8
- SOR/2006-270, s. 6
- SOR/2014-162, ss. 100, 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-312-20081215.html "Link to previous version of section 312"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Communications]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710290 .Subheading}

- **[[313]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-313 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  marine facility security officer shall have the means to notify all
  marine facility personnel of changes in security conditions at the
  marine facility.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The marine facility shall have communication systems
  and procedures that allow effective communications

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} between the marine facility security personnel and
    vessels interfacing with the marine facility and, if the marine
    facility is in a port, the port administration; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the Minister and local law enforcement
    agencies.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The communication systems shall have a backup to
  ensure internal and external communications.

### [Coordination of Security Procedures during Interfacing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710297 .Subheading}

**[[314]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-314 .sectionLabel}** The operator of a
marine facility shall ensure that the implementation of the marine
facility security procedures is coordinated with vessels interfacing
with the marine facility and, if the marine facility is in a port, with
the port administration.

### [Declaration of Security]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710299 .Subheading}

- **[[315]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-315 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  declaration of security shall be completed before an interface starts
  between a marine facility and a vessel if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are operating at different MARSEC levels;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one of them does not have a security plan approved
    by a contracting government;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the interface involves a cruise ship, a vessel
    carrying certain dangerous cargoes or the loading or transfer of
    certain dangerous cargoes; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the security officer of either of them identifies
    security concerns about the interface.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A new declaration of security is required if there is
  a change in the MARSEC level.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The declaration of security shall provide a means for
  ensuring that all shared security concerns are fully taken into
  account throughout the interface and shall contain the information set
  out in the form in Appendix 1 of Part B of the ISPS Code, with the
  terms "ship", "port facility" and "security measures" read as
  "vessel", "marine facility" and "security procedures", respectively.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The declaration of security shall be in English or
  French and be signed by the marine facility security officer and the
  vessel security officer.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A marine facility security officer or vessel security
  officer may authorize in writing a person who has security
  responsibilities at the marine facility or on the vessel and
  appropriate training to complete and sign the declaration of security
  on their behalf.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} At MARSEC level 1 and MARSEC level 2, a continuing
  declaration of security may be used for multiple interfaces between a
  marine facility and a vessel if the effective period of the
  declaration does not exceed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 90 days at MARSEC level 1; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 30 days at MARSEC level 2.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} If a declaration of security is required under
  subsection (1) between a vessel and the operator of a lock in the St.
  Lawrence Seaway, it shall be completed on its entry into the first
  lock and remain in effect until the vessel exits the St. Lawrence
  Seaway at the St. Lambert Lock or the Welland Canal at Port Colborne.

### [Marine Facility Security Assessments]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710314 .Subheading}

#### [Requirements for Persons Providing Security Assessment Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710315 .Subheading}

**[[316]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-316 .sectionLabel}** The persons who provide
security assessment information shall have, collectively, the competence
to evaluate the security of the marine facility, including knowledge in
the following areas:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} current security threats and patterns;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the detection and recognition of weapons, explosives
  and incendiaries and other dangerous substances and devices;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
  behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} techniques that might be used to violate security
  procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment or systems;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} methods used to cause a security incident;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the effects of dangerous substances and devices on
  structures and essential services;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} marine facility and vessel interface business
  practices;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} emergency preparedness and response and contingency
  planning;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} physical security requirements;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} radio and telecommunications systems, including
  computer systems and networks;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} marine or civil engineering; and

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} marine facility and vessel operations.

#### [Security Assessment Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710329 .Subheading}

- **[[317]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-317
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Security assessment information in
  respect of a marine facility

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be in English or French;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall be based on background information, the
    completion of an on-site survey and an analysis of that information
    and survey;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall identify and evaluate

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the physical aspects of the marine facility that
      are the most important to protect and the means for protecting the
      personnel,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} possible threats to the marine facility and the
      likelihood of their occurrence, in order to establish and
      prioritize security procedures and countermeasures, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the vulnerabilities, including human factors,
      in the security of the marine facility; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} may cover more than one marine facility.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Security assessment information shall consist of the
  following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the general layout of the marine facility,
    including the location of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} active and inactive access points to the marine
      facility,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} security doors, barriers, and lighting,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} restricted areas,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} emergency and stand-by equipment available to
      maintain essential services,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} storage areas for maintenance equipment, ships'
      stores, cargo and unaccompanied baggage,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} escape and evacuation routes and assembly
      stations, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} existing security and safety equipment for the
      protection of personnel and visitors;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} changes in the tide that might have an impact on
    the vulnerability or security of the marine facility;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a list of the emergency and stand-by equipment
    available to maintain essential services;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the number of marine facility personnel, the
    security tasks of persons with security responsibilities and the
    training requirements and procedures of the marine facility;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a list of existing security and safety equipment
    for the protection of marine facility personnel and visitors;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} escape and evacuation routes and assembly stations
    that have to be maintained to ensure the orderly and safe emergency
    evacuation of the marine facility;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the results of security audits; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} security procedures in effect, including inspection
    and control procedures, identification systems, surveillance and
    monitoring equipment, personnel identification documents and
    communication, alarm, lighting, access control and other appropriate
    systems.

#### [Elements of Security Assessments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710355 .Subheading}

**[[318]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-318 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
conduct the marine facility security assessment, which addresses the
following elements in respect of the marine facility, as applicable:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the physical security;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the structural integrity;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} personnel protection systems;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} operational procedures that might impact on security;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} its radio and telecommunications systems, including
  computer systems and networks;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} relevant transportation support infrastructure;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} utilities; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} other elements that might, if damaged or used
  illicitly, pose a risk to people, property or operations at the marine
  facility.

#### [Matters to be Taken into Account in Security Assessments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710365 .Subheading}

**[[319]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-319 .sectionLabel}** A marine facility
security assessment shall take into account potential threats and the
following types of security incidents:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} damage to, or destruction of, the marine facility or
  a vessel by explosive devices, arson, sabotage or vandalism;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} tampering with essential equipment or systems, ships'
  stores or cargo of the marine facility;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} unauthorized access to the marine facility;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the smuggling onto the marine facility of weapons or
  equipment, including weapons of mass destruction;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} use of the marine facility itself as a weapon or as a
  means to cause damage or destruction;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} nuclear, biological, radiological, explosive or
  chemical attacks on the shoreside support system of the marine
  facility or on a vessel interfacing with the marine facility;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the seizure of the marine facility or the seizure or
  hijacking of an interfacing vessel or persons on board; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} use of the marine facility or its equipment by
  persons intending to cause a security incident.

#### [On-site Survey and Vulnerability Assessments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710375 .Subheading}

**[[320]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-320 .sectionLabel}** The operator of a
marine facility shall ensure that an on-site survey of the marine
facility is conducted. The survey shall examine and evaluate current
protective procedures and operations to verify or collect security
assessment information.

- **[[321]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-321 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  marine facility security assessment shall include a vulnerability
  assessment undertaken in consultation with the operator of the marine
  facility to determine the following so as to produce an overall
  assessment of the level of risk for which security procedures have to
  be developed:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any particular aspect of the marine facility,
    including vessel traffic in the vicinity, that might make it a
    target of an attack;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the potential consequences of an attack on or at
    the marine facility in terms of loss of life, damage to property and
    economic disruption, including the disruption of marine transport
    systems;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the capability and intent of those likely to mount
    an attack; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the potential types of attack.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vulnerability assessment shall include a
  consideration of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} current security procedures, including
    identification systems;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} methods and points of access to the marine
    facility;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to protect radio and
    telecommunications equipment, including computer systems and
    networks;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any conflicting policies between safety and
    security procedures;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any enforcement or personnel constraints;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} methods of monitoring restricted areas and other
    areas that have restricted access to ensure that only authorized
    persons have access;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} areas adjacent to the marine facility that might be
    exploited during or for an attack;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} current security procedures relating to utilities
    and other services;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any deficiencies identified during training or
    drills;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} any deficiencies identified during daily operations
    or following incidents or alerts, reports of security concerns, the
    application of control measures or audits; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the structural integrity of the marine facility.

### [Marine Facility Security Plan]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710395 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710396 .Subheading}

**[[322]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-322 .sectionLabel}** A marine facility
security plan

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be based on the findings of the marine facility
  security assessment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall be in English or French;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall be submitted to the Minister for approval;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} is valid for a period determined by the Minister,
  which shall not exceed five years after the date of the Minister's
  approval; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} may cover more than one marine facility if they share
  similarities in physical characteristics, location and operations.

#### [Content]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710403 .Subheading}

**[[323]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-323 .sectionLabel}** A marine facility
security plan shall address each vulnerability identified in the marine
facility security assessment and include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the organization of the marine facility in terms of
  security, including the tasks of personnel who have security
  responsibilities;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the operator and the name and position of
  the marine facility security officer, including information on how
  they may be contacted at any time;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the identification of restricted areas and any
  security procedures, equipment and systems for those areas;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the procedures for and the frequency
  of drills and exercises;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of procedures for the following:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} ensuring the security of information in the marine
    facility security plan and keeping the records referred to in
    section 312,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} maintaining security and communication systems and
    equipment,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} identifying and correcting security equipment or
    systems failures or malfunctions,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} communications,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} responding to changes in the MARSEC level,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} interfacing with vessels at each MARSEC level,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} declarations of security,

  - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} preventing unauthorized weapons, explosives,
    incendiaries or other dangerous substances or devices from entering
    the marine facility,

  - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} reporting security threats, security breaches and
    security incidents to the appropriate law enforcement agencies, the
    Minister and, if applicable, the port administration,

  - [(x)]{.lawlabel} securing non-critical operations in order to focus
    response on critical operations, and

  - [(xi)]{.lawlabel} periodically reviewing, updating and auditing the
    marine facility security plan;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a description of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} security procedures, equipment and systems for
    access control,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} security procedures for the delivery of ships'
    stores and bunkers,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} security procedures, equipment and systems for
    monitoring the marine facility and surrounding area, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} procedures for security threats, security breaches
    and security incidents, including procedures for the evacuation of
    the marine facility;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the rate at which inspections of the marine facility
  are conducted; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the identification of positions for which a
  transportation security clearance is required.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 9
- SOR/2014-162, ss. 100, 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-323-20081215.html "Link to previous version of section 323"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [MARSEC Level Coordination and Implementation of Security Procedures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710430 .Subheading}

- **[[324]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-324 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  marine facility security plan shall contain security procedures for
  ensuring that, when the operator of the marine facility is notified of
  an increase in the MARSEC level,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility complies with the required
    additional security procedures within 12 hours after the
    notification;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a report indicating compliance or noncompliance
    with the MARSEC level is made to the Minister; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessels interfacing with the marine facility
    and the vessels scheduled to arrive at the marine facility within 96
    hours after the MARSEC level is increased are notified of the new
    MARSEC level and the declaration of security is revised accordingly.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The marine facility security plan shall contain
  security procedures to ensure that, when notified of an increase in
  the MARSEC level, the marine facility security officer informs all
  marine facility personnel of identified security threats, emphasizes
  reporting procedures and stresses the need for increased vigilance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For MARSEC levels 2 and 3, the marine facility
  security plan shall contain procedures for ensuring that the operator
  of the marine facility evaluates the need for additional security
  procedures.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 32
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-324-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 324"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Security Procedures for Access Control]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710440 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710441 .Subheading}

- **[[325]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-325 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  marine facility security plan shall contain security procedures, as
  appropriate to the facility's operations, to control access to the
  marine facility at each MARSEC level and to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} deter the unauthorized entry of weapons, explosives
    and incendiaries, including any device that could be used to damage
    or destroy marine facilities or vessels or harm individuals;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} secure any weapons, explosives, incendiaries or
    other dangerous substances and devices that are authorized by the
    operator to be at the marine facility;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} identify the locations at which restrictions or
    prohibitions preventing unauthorized access are to be applied for
    each MARSEC level and that each location allowing access to the
    marine facility is addressed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} identify the types of restrictions or prohibitions
    to be applied and the means of enforcing them;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} establish the means of identification required to
    allow persons and vehicles to have access to or to remain in the
    marine facility without challenge;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} identify the locations at which the authorized
    screening of persons and goods, including vehicles, is to be
    conducted, and to ensure that these locations are covered to enable
    continuous screenings regardless of weather conditions; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} facilitate shore leave for vessel personnel or crew
    change.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The plan shall contain security procedures for
  verification of the identity of marine facility personnel and other
  persons seeking access to the marine facility that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} allow identification of authorized persons at each
    MARSEC level;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are coordinated, to the extent practicable, with
    the identification systems of vessels that interface with the marine
    facility;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are updated regularly; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} allow temporary or continuing access to the marine
    facility by marine facility personnel, vessel personnel and other
    authorized persons, through the use of a badge or other means of
    verifying their identity.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The plan shall set out the frequency of application
  of access controls, particularly if they are to be applied on a random
  or occasional basis.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 33
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-325-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 325"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710459 .Subheading}

**[[326]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-326 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 1, the
security procedures for access control shall include, as appropriate to
the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} verifying the identity of every person seeking to
  enter a controlled access area and the reasons for which they seek
  entry by confirming at least one of the following:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} joining instructions,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} passenger tickets,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} boarding passes,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} work orders or marine surveyor orders,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} government identification,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} restricted area passes,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} access passes or other identification issued by
    the marine facility or, if applicable, passes issued by the port
    administration, or

  - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} visitor badges issued in accordance with an
    identification system;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorized screening, at the rate specified in the
  marine facility security plan, of persons and goods, including
  vehicles, for weapons, explosives or incendiaries;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} denying or revoking access to a marine facility by
  persons who are unable or unwilling, at the request of marine facility
  personnel, to establish their identity or account for their presence
  at the marine facility and recording details of the denials and
  revocations;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} determining the appropriate access controls for
  restricted areas;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} identifying access points that must be secured or
  attended to deter unauthorized access; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} deterring unauthorized access to the marine facility
  and to restricted areas.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710475 .Subheading}

**[[327]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-327 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 2, the
additional security procedures for access control shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} increasing the frequency and detail of the authorized
  screening of persons and goods, including vehicles, entering the
  marine facility, for weapons, explosives and incendiaries;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} X-ray screening of all unaccompanied baggage for
  weapons, explosives and incendiaries;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} assigning additional personnel to guard access points
  and to patrol the perimeter of the marine facility to deter
  unauthorized access;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} limiting the number of access points to the marine
  facility by closing and securing some access points and providing
  physical barriers to impede movement through the remaining access
  points;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} denying or revoking access by persons who are unable
  to provide a verifiable justification for seeking access to the marine
  facility; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} coordinating with the Minister, the appropriate law
  enforcement agencies and, if the marine facility is in a port, the
  port administration for the deterrence of waterside access to the
  marine facility, including using waterborne patrols to enhance
  security around the marine facility and any vessels located there.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710483 .Subheading}

**[[328]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-328 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 3, the
additional security procedures for access control shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conducting the authorized screening of all persons
  and goods, including vehicles, for weapons, explosives and
  incendiaries;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of unaccompanied baggage,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} conducting authorized screening more extensively
    for weapons, explosives and incendiaries, for example, by X-raying
    from two or more angles,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} restricting or suspending the handling of it, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} refusing to accept it;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} cooperating with emergency response personnel and
  other marine facilities;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} granting access only to those responding to a
  security incident or security threat;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} suspending access to the marine facility;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} suspending cargo operations;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} evacuating the marine facility;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} restricting pedestrian or vehicular movement on the
  grounds of the marine facility; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} increasing monitoring at the marine facility.

### [Security Procedures for Restricted Areas]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710497 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710498 .Subheading}

- **[[329]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-329 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  marine facility security plan shall designate restricted areas, as
  appropriate to the facility's operations, in order to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevent or deter unauthorized access;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} protect the marine facility, including security and
    surveillance equipment and systems, and persons authorized to be at
    the facility;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} protect vessels interfacing with the marine
    facility; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} protect ships' stores from tampering.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The plan shall contain security procedures to ensure
  that notices are posted in compliance with section 21 of the Act.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Restricted areas shall be established for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} land areas adjacent to vessels interfacing with the
    marine facility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} areas in which security-sensitive information is
    kept, including cargo documentation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} areas containing the central controls for security
    and surveillance equipment and systems and areas that contain the
    central lighting system controls;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} areas containing the critical infrastructure of the
    marine facility, such as

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} water supplies,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} telecommunications,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} electrical systems, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} access points for ventilation and
      air-conditioning systems;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} manufacturing or processing areas and control
    rooms;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} locations in the marine facility where it is
    reasonable to restrict access by vehicles and persons;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} areas designated for loading, unloading or storage
    of cargo and ships' stores; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} areas containing certain dangerous cargoes.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The following areas shall be established as
  restricted area two:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} areas in the marine facilities set out in Part 1 of
    Schedule 1 that contain the central controls for security and
    surveillance equipment and systems and areas that contain the
    central lighting system controls; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} areas that are designated for the loading or
    unloading of cargo and ships' stores at the cruise ship terminals
    set out in Part 1 of Schedule 1 and land areas adjacent to vessels
    interfacing with those cruise ship terminals.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-329-20061102.html "Link to previous version of section 329"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[330]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-330 .sectionLabel}** A marine facility
security plan shall contain security procedures, as appropriate to the
facility's operations, for restricted areas at each MARSEC level for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} identifying the marine facility personnel and other
  persons who are authorized to have access;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} determining the conditions under which access may
  take place including procedures for escorting persons who do not have
  restricted area passes;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} establishing the times during which access
  restrictions apply; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensuring that notices are posted in compliance with
  section 21 of the Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 23(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-330-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 330"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710531 .Subheading}

- **[[331]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-331 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  MARSEC level 1, a marine facility security plan shall contain security
  procedures, as appropriate to the facility's operations, to prevent
  unauthorized access or activities in restricted areas that include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} restricting access to authorized personnel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} securing all access points not actively used and
    providing physical barriers to impede movement through the remaining
    access points;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} controlling access to restricted areas;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} examining the identification and authorization of
    persons and vehicles seeking entry;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} patrolling or monitoring the perimeter of
    restricted areas;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} using security personnel, automatic intrusion
    detection devices, surveillance equipment or surveillance systems to
    detect unauthorized entry into or movement in restricted areas;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} directing the parking, loading and unloading of
    vehicles in restricted areas;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} controlling the movement and storage of cargo and
    ships' stores;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} designating restricted areas for performing
    inspections of cargo and ships' stores that are awaiting loading;
    and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} designating temporary restricted areas to
    accommodate marine facility operations, including restricted areas
    for segregating unaccompanied baggage that has undergone authorized
    screening by a vessel operator.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The marine facility security plan shall include a
  requirement that, if a temporary restricted area is designated, a
  security sweep of it shall be conducted both before and after it is
  designated.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710545 .Subheading}

**[[332]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-332 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 2,
additional security procedures for restricted areas shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} increasing the intensity and frequency of monitoring
  and access control of restricted areas;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} enhancing the effectiveness of the physical barriers
  surrounding restricted areas, by the use of patrols or automatic
  intrusion detection devices;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} reducing the number of access points to restricted
  areas and enhancing the controls applied at the remaining access
  points;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} restricting the parking of vehicles adjacent to
  vessels;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} reducing access to the restricted areas and movements
  and storage in them;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} using surveillance equipment that records and
  monitors continuously;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} increasing the number and frequency of patrols,
  including the use of waterborne patrols; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} establishing and restricting access to areas adjacent
  to restricted areas.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710555 .Subheading}

**[[333]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-333 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 3, the
additional security procedures for restricted areas shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} designating additional restricted areas;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting access to restricted areas; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} searching restricted areas as part of a security
  sweep of all or part of the marine facility.

### [Security Procedures for Handling Cargo]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710560 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710561 .Subheading}

**[[334]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334 .sectionLabel}** A marine facility
security plan shall contain security procedures, as appropriate to the
facility's operations, for cargo handling for each MARSEC level for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} deterring tampering and detecting evidence of it;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} preventing cargo that is not meant for carriage from
  being accepted or stored at the marine facility without the consent of
  the operator of the marine facility;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} identifying cargo that is accepted for loading onto
  vessels interfacing with the marine facility;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} controlling inventory at access points to the marine
  facility;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} identifying cargo that is accepted for temporary
  storage in a restricted area while awaiting loading or pick up;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} releasing cargo only to the carrier specified in the
  cargo documentation;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} coordinating with shippers and other persons
  responsible for cargo;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} creating, updating, and maintaining a continuous
  inventory of certain dangerous cargoes, from receipt to delivery in
  the marine facility, that sets out the location in which they are
  stored; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the examination of the documentation of cargo
  entering the marine facility.

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710572 .Subheading}

**[[335]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-335 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 1, the
security procedures for cargo handling shall include, as appropriate to
the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} verifying that cargo, containers and cargo transport
  units entering the marine facility match the invoice or other cargo
  documentation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} routinely inspecting cargo, containers, cargo
  transport units and cargo storage areas in the marine facility before
  and during cargo handling operations to detect evidence of tampering,
  unless it is unsafe to do so;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} examining documents for vehicles entering the marine
  facility; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} examining seals and other methods used to detect
  evidence of tampering when cargo, containers or cargo transport units
  enter the marine facility or are stored there.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710578 .Subheading}

**[[336]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-336 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 2, the
additional security procedures for cargo handling shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} authorized screening of cargo, containers, and cargo
  transport units in or about to enter the marine facility and cargo
  storage areas for weapons, explosives and incendiaries;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} intensifying inspections to ensure that only
  documented cargo enters the marine facility, is temporarily stored
  there and is then loaded on board a vessel;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorized screening of vehicles for weapons,
  explosives and incendiaries;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} increasing the frequency and detail of examinations
  of seals and other methods used to prevent tampering;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} segregating inbound cargo, outbound cargo and ships'
  stores;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} increasing the frequency and intensity of visual and
  physical inspections; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} limiting the number of locations where certain
  dangerous cargoes are stored.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710587 .Subheading}

**[[337]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-337 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 3, the
additional security procedures for cargo handling shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} restricting or suspending cargo movements or
  operations in all or part of the marine facility;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} cooperating with responders and vessels; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} confirming the inventory and location of certain
  dangerous cargoes in the marine facility.

### [Security Procedures for Delivery of Ships' Stores and Bunkers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710592 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710593 .Subheading}

**[[338]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-338 .sectionLabel}** A marine facility
security plan shall contain security procedures, as appropriate to the
facility's operations, for the delivery of ships' stores and bunkers for
each MARSEC level for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} inspecting ships' stores for package integrity;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} preventing them from being accepted without
  inspection; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} preventing tampering.

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710598 .Subheading}

**[[339]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-339 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 1, the
security procedures for ships' stores and bunkers shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} coordinating with vessel operators that are
  inspecting ships' stores;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} requiring advance notification of the delivery of
  ships' stores or bunkers, including a list of stores or bunkers, and
  driver and vehicle registration information in respect of the delivery
  vehicle;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} inspecting delivery vehicles at the rate specified in
  the marine facility security plan; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} controlling the delivery vehicles in the marine
  facility.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710604 .Subheading}

**[[340]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-340 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 2, the
additional security procedures for ships' stores and bunkers shall
include, as appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} coordinating with vessel operators that are
  inspecting ships' stores more intensively;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorized screening of delivery vehicles for
  weapons, explosives and incendiaries at the rate specified in the
  marine facility security plan;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} verifying that ships' stores and bunkers, before they
  enter the marine facility, match the invoice or other documentation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} escorting delivery vehicles in the marine facility;
  or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} restricting or prohibiting the entry of ships' stores
  and bunkers that will not leave the marine facility within the period
  of time set out in the invoice or other documentation.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710611 .Subheading}

**[[341]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-341 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 3, the
additional security procedures for the delivery of ships' stores and
bunkers shall include, as appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} authorized screening of all delivery vehicles for
  weapons, explosives and incendiaries;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} restricting or suspending the delivery of ships'
  stores and bunkers; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} refusing to accept ships' stores and bunkers in the
  marine facility.

### [Security Procedures for Monitoring]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710616 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710617 .Subheading}

- **[[342]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-342 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  marine facility security plan shall contain, as appropriate to the
  facility's operations, security procedures for each MARSEC level for
  the continuous monitoring of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility and its approaches on land and
    water;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} restricted areas in the marine facility; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} vessels interfacing with the marine facility.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The plan may provide that the monitoring may be
  effected by any combination of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} lighting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} security guards, on foot or in vehicles, and
    waterborne patrols; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} automatic intrusion-detection devices and
    surveillance equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following shall be ensured when establishing the
  appropriate level and location of lighting:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} marine facility personnel are able to detect
    activities at the marine facility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the lighting facilitates the identification of
    persons at access points; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the lighting can be provided through coordination
    with the port administration and vessels.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The plan shall provide that monitoring equipment

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if it is an automatic intrusion-detection device,
    activates an audible or visual alarm, or both, at a location that is
    continuously attended or monitored;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is able to function continuously, including during
    periods of adverse weather or power disruption;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} monitors access and movements adjacent to vessels
    interfacing with the marine facility; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} limits lighting effects, such as glare, and their
    impact on safety, navigation and other security activities.

#### [MARSEC Level 1]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710636 .Subheading}

**[[343]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-343 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 1, the
security procedures shall provide, as appropriate to the facility's
operations, for monitoring at all times.

#### [MARSEC Level 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710638 .Subheading}

**[[344]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-344 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 2, the
additional security procedures for monitoring shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} increasing the coverage and intensity of lighting and
  surveillance equipment, including the provision of additional lighting
  and surveillance;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} increasing the frequency of foot, vehicle or
  waterborne patrols; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} assigning additional security personnel to monitor
  and patrol.

#### [MARSEC Level 3]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710643 .Subheading}

**[[345]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-345 .sectionLabel}** For MARSEC level 3, the
additional security procedures for monitoring shall include, as
appropriate to the facility's operations,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} switching on all lighting in, or illuminating the
  vicinity of, the marine facility;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} switching on all surveillance equipment capable of
  recording activities in or adjacent to the marine facility; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} maximizing the length of time that surveillance
  equipment can continue to record.

#### [Security Threats, Security Breaches and Security Incidents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710648 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2014-162, ss. 34(E), 101(E)

\]
:::

**[[346]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-346 .sectionLabel}** At each MARSEC level,
the marine security plan shall contain procedures, as appropriate to the
facility's operations, for the marine facility security officer and
persons who have security responsibilities with respect to the facility
to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} respond to security threats, security breaches and
  security incidents and maintain critical marine facility and interface
  operations, including by

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting entry into the affected area,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} denying access to the marine facility, except to
    persons responding to the threat, breach or incident,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} implementing MARSEC level 3 security procedures
    throughout the marine facility,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} stopping cargo-handling operations, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} notifying shoreside authorities or vessels of the
    threat or incident;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} evacuate the marine facility in case of security
  threats and security incidents;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} report any security threats, security breaches or
  security incidents to the Minister without undue delay;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} brief marine facility personnel on potential threats
  to security and the need for vigilance and their assistance in
  reporting suspicious persons, objects or activities; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} secure non-critical operations in order to focus
  response on critical operations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, ss. 35, 101(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-346-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 346"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Additional Passenger Facility and Ferry Facility Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710664 .Subheading}

- **[[347]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-347 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  MARSEC level 1, the marine facility security plan in respect of a
  passenger facility or a ferry facility shall contain security
  procedures additional to those required in sections 324 to 345 for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a marine facility with no designated public
    access area, establishing separate areas to segregate persons and
    goods, including vehicles, that have not been screened from those
    that have;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a marine facility with a designated public
    access area, providing sufficient security personnel to monitor all
    persons in the area and to conduct authorized screening of persons
    and goods;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorized screening of vehicles to be loaded on
    board a vessel for weapons, explosives and incendiaries before
    loading;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} authorized screening of all unaccompanied vehicles
    to be loaded on board a vessel for weapons, explosives and
    incendiaries before loading; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} denying passenger access to restricted areas unless
    the restricted areas are monitored by marine facility security
    personnel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For MARSEC level 2, the marine facility security plan
  in respect of a passenger facility or ferry facility that has no
  designated public access area shall contain additional security
  procedures for the authorized screening of passengers and goods,
  including vehicles, before they board or are loaded on board a vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For MARSEC level 3, the marine facility security plan
  of a passenger facility or ferry facility with no designated public
  access area shall contain additional security procedures for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} verifying the identity of all persons and
    conducting authorized screening of them for weapons, explosives and
    incendiaries;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} conducting authorized screening of all goods,
    including vehicles, for weapons, explosives and incendiaries; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} assigning additional security personnel and
    patrols.

#### [Additional Requirements for Cruise Ship Terminals]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710677 .Subheading}

**[[348]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-348 .sectionLabel}** For each MARSEC level,
the marine facility security plan in respect of a cruise ship terminal
shall contain security procedures additional to those required in
sections 324 to 345 for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} verifying the identity of all persons seeking to
  enter a controlled access area of the cruise ship terminal by, for
  example, confirming the reason for boarding or examining joining
  instructions, passenger tickets, boarding passes, government
  identification, visitor badges or work orders;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} assisting the operator of a vessel to coordinate the
  authorized screening of all persons and goods, including vehicles, for
  weapons, explosives and incendiaries;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} designating holding, waiting and embarkation areas to
  segregate persons and baggage that have been screened and are awaiting
  embarkation from those that have not been screened;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} providing additional security personnel to designated
  holding, waiting, and embarkation areas; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} denying passenger access to restricted areas.

#### [Additional Requirements for CDC Facilities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710684 .Subheading}

- **[[349]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-349 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  each MARSEC level, the marine facility security plan in respect of a
  CDC facility shall contain security procedures additional to those
  required in sections 324 to 345 for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of prearranged cargo deliveries,
    escorting all persons who are not personnel of the CDC facility at
    all times while they are at the CDC facility if they do not provide
    access identification;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} controlling the parking and loading and unloading
    of vehicles;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} requiring security personnel to record or report
    their presence at key points during their patrols;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} conducting a security sweep of unmanned or
    unmonitored waterfront areas for dangerous substances and devices
    before a vessel's arrival; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} providing an alternative or independent power
    source for security and communication systems.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For MARSEC level 2, the additional security
  procedures shall include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} releasing cargo only in the presence of the marine
    facility security officer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} continuously guarding or patrolling restricted
    areas.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For MARSEC level 3, the marine facility security plan
  shall contain additional security procedures to ensure that the CDC
  facility is continuously guarded and the restricted areas are
  patrolled.

**[[350]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-350 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2014-162, s. 36\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-350-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 350"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Audits and Amendments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710697 .Subheading}

- **[[351]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-351 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  audit of a marine facility security plan shall take into account the
  most recent marine facility security assessment and determine whether
  there are any deficiencies or changes in security threats, procedures,
  responsibilities of personnel, operations or operator that require
  amendments to be made to the plan.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An audit is conducted

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} annually, effective on the day on which the plan is
    approved; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whenever there is a new operator of the marine
    facility, a change in operations or location or modifications to the
    marine facility that could affect its security.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Persons conducting an audit shall have knowledge of
  the methods of conducting audits and inspections and of access control
  and monitoring techniques and, if possible, be independent of the
  activities being audited.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the results of an audit require an amendment to be
  made to the marine facility security assessment or plan, the marine
  facility security officer shall submit an amendment to the Minister
  for approval within 30 days after completion of the audit.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a marine facility may submit to the
  Minister other amendments to the approved marine facility security
  plan. They shall be submitted at least 30 days before they are to take
  effect.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a marine facility shall, within 60
  days after the day on which the Minister informs the operator in
  writing that the marine facility security plan no longer meets the
  requirements to remain approved, ensure that amendments are submitted
  to the Minister for approval.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} If amendments are required under subsection (4)
  or (6) and they are not submitted or the amended plan is not approved,
  the plan ceases to be valid on the day on which the operator of the
  marine facility receives notification that the plan is no longer
  valid.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} The marine facility security officer shall attach to
  the plan an approval document issued under subsection 352(1).

#### [Submission and Approval]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710709 .Subheading}

- **[[352]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-352 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister is satisfied that a marine facility security plan meets
  the requirements of this Part, the Minister shall approve the plan and
  issue a letter of approval certifying that the plan meets the
  requirements of this Part, unless approving the plan is not in the
  public interest and is likely to adversely affect marine
  transportation security.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 351(7), a plan is valid for the
  period fixed by the Minister, not exceeding five years after the day
  on which it is approved by the Minister. In fixing the period, the
  Minister shall consider the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility's operations and the industry
    in which it operates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operator's security record;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility's security record;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the complexity of the marine facility security plan
    and the details of its procedures; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the findings of the marine facility security
    assessment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall issue, in English or French, an
  interim statement of compliance in respect of a marine facility if the
  marine facility security plan has been approved but a security
  inspector has not yet conducted an inspection under section 23 of the
  Act to ensure compliance with the requirements of this Part.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The interim statement of compliance is valid until
  the expiry of six months after the day on which it is issued or until
  a statement of compliance is issued under subsection (5), whichever
  comes first.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is satisfied, on the basis of an
  inspection conducted under section 23 of the Act, that the
  requirements of this Part have been met, the Minister shall issue, in
  English or French, a statement of compliance in respect of the marine
  facility.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The interim statement of compliance and the statement
  of compliance are valid while the approved security plan in respect of
  the marine facility is valid.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 37
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-352-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 352"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Required Documentation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710724 .Subheading}

**[[353]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-353 .sectionLabel}** No person shall operate
a marine facility, except an occasional-use marine facility, without a
valid interim statement of compliance issued under subsection 352(3) or
a valid statement of compliance issued under subsection 352(5).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 38
:::

**\[354 reserved\]**

### [Occasional-Use Marine Facilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710729 .Subheading}

#### [Operator]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710730 .Subheading}

**[[355]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-355 .sectionLabel}** The operator of an
occasional-use marine facility shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish an administrative and organizational
  structure for security at the marine facility;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide every person who has responsibilities under
  this Part with the support needed to fulfil their responsibilities;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} designate in writing an occasional-use marine
  facility security officer by name or by a position other than the
  position for which the designation is being made;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the implementation of the marine facility
  security procedures is coordinated with vessels interfacing with the
  marine facility;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} coordinate, with the master of a vessel and, if
  applicable, the port security officer, shore leave for vessel
  personnel or crew change, as well as access through the marine
  facility of visitors to vessels, including representatives of
  seafarers' welfare and labour organizations;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} provide information respecting the security of the
  facility to those persons who need it to comply with these
  Regulations; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} clearly mark with signs each restricted area at the
  marine facility.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 39
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-355-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 355"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Occasional-Use Marine Facility Security Officer]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710741 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710742 .Subheading}

- **[[356]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-356 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  occasional-use marine facility security officer may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} act in that capacity for more than one
    occasional-use marine facility if they are able to fulfil the
    responsibilities for each occasional-use marine facility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have other responsibilities within the operator's
    organization if they are able to fulfil their responsibilities; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} delegate tasks required by section 358.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An occasional-use marine facility security officer
  remains responsible for the performance of the tasks they delegate.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-269, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-356-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 356"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Qualifications]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710751 .Subheading}

**[[357]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-357 .sectionLabel}** An occasional-use
marine facility security officer shall have, by training or job
experience, knowledge and competence that is relevant to the industry in
which the marine facility operates and is in the following areas:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the administrative and organizational structure for
  security at the occasional-use marine facility;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operations and operating conditions of the
  occasional-use marine facility and vessels;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the security procedures of the occasional-use marine
  facility, vessels and port, including the meaning and the requirements
  of the different MARSEC levels;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} emergency preparedness and response and contingency
  planning;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} security equipment and systems and their operational
  limitations;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} relevant international conventions, codes, standards
  and recommendations;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} relevant legislation, regulations and security
  measures, rules and procedures; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the responsibilities and functions of municipal,
  provincial and federal law enforcement agencies.

#### [Responsibilities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710761 .Subheading}

**[[358]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-358 .sectionLabel}** An occasional-use
marine facility security officer shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure security awareness and vigilance at the
  occasional-use marine facility, including awareness of changes in the
  MARSEC level and other circumstances that might affect work conditions
  there;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that appropriate security training is provided
  to personnel at the occasional-use marine facility in accordance with
  this Part;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} report security incidents to the appropriate law
  enforcement agencies and the Minister as soon as possible after they
  occur so that an investigation can be conducted;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} coordinate the signing and implementation of the
  declaration of security between the occasional-use marine facility and
  interfacing vessel;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} shall, in the case of an interface with a vessel to
  which Part 2 applies, ensure that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} security sweeps are performed before and after the
    interface in order to confirm the absence of security threats,
    dangerous substances or devices, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} implement any temporary security procedures that
    are required during the interface; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} keep records of security sweeps and declarations of
  security in respect of the occasional-use marine facility and send a
  copy of each declaration of security to the Minister.

#### [Security Procedures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710771 .Subheading}

- **[[359]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-359
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Security procedures shall be
  established for an occasional-use marine facility. The security
  procedures shall include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the organization of the
    occasional-use marine facility in terms of security, including the
    tasks of marine facility personnel with security responsibilities;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the operator and the name and position
    of the occasional-use marine facility security officer, including
    information on how they may be contacted at any time;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of procedures for:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} responding to changes in the MARSEC level,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} interfacing, at each MARSEC level, with vessels
      to which Part 2 applies,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} coordinating, with the master of a vessel and,
      if applicable, the port security officer, shore leave for vessel
      personnel or crew change, as well as access to the vessel by
      visitors passing through the occasional-use marine facility,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} reporting security incidents to the appropriate
      law enforcement agencies, the Minister and, if applicable, the
      port administration,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} meeting the requirements respecting declarations
      of security,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} implementing any temporary security procedures
      that are required during an interface,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} keeping the records referred to in paragraph
      358(f),

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} as appropriate to the occasional-use marine
      facility's operations, briefing marine facility personnel on
      potential threats to security and on the need for vigilance and
      for their assistance in reporting suspicious persons, objects or
      activities, at each MARSEC level, and

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} as appropriate to the occasional-use marine
      facility's operations, performing the security sweeps required by
      paragraph 358(e).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The security procedures established for an
  occasional-use marine facility shall also include procedures, as
  appropriate to the marine facility's operations, to control access to
  the marine facility at each MARSEC level and to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} deter the unauthorized entry of weapons, explosives
    and incendiaries, including any device that could be used to damage
    or destroy marine facilities or vessels or harm individuals;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} secure any weapons, explosives, incendiaries or
    other dangerous substances and devices that are authorized by the
    operator to be at the occasional-use marine facility;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} identify the locations at which restrictions or
    prohibitions to prevent unauthorized access are to be applied for
    each MARSEC level and specify that each location allowing access to
    the occasional-use marine facility is to be addressed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} identify the types of restrictions or prohibitions
    to be applied and the means of applying them;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} establish the means of identification required to
    allow persons and vehicles to have access to or remain in the
    occasional-use marine facility without challenge; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} identify the locations at which the authorized
    screening of persons and goods, including vehicles, is to be
    conducted, and ensure that these locations are covered in order to
    enable continuous screening regardless of weather conditions.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 40
:::

#### [Approval of Security Procedures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710795 .Subheading}

- **[[360]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-360 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister is satisfied that the security procedures for an
  occasional-use marine facility meet the requirements of section 359,
  the Minister shall approve the security procedures and issue a letter
  of compliance certifying that they meet the requirements of this Part,
  unless approving the security procedures is not in the public interest
  and is likely to adversely affect marine transportation security.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Security procedures are valid for the period fixed by
  the Minister, which shall not exceed five years after the day on which
  they are approved by the Minister. In fixing the period, the Minister
  shall consider the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility's operations and the industry
    in which it operates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operator's security record;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the marine facility's security record; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the complexity of the security procedures of the
    marine facility and the details of those procedures.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 40
:::

#### [Required Documentation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710805 .Subheading}

**[[360.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-360.1 .sectionLabel}** No person shall
operate an occasional-use marine facility without a valid letter of
compliance issued under subsection 360(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 40
:::

### [Ports]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710809 .Subheading}

**[[361]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-361 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2014-162, s. 41\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-361-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 361"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Responsibilities of the Port Administration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710811 .Subheading}

**[[362]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-362 .sectionLabel}** A port administration
shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish, convene, and direct a port security
  committee;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} designate in writing, by name, a port security
  officer who will chair the port security committee;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} conduct an on-site survey and submit to the Minister
  the security assessment information respecting the port;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide the Minister with the information that is
  necessary to conduct a vulnerability assessment;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} submit a port security plan, and any amendment, to
  the Minister for approval;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} ensure the coordination of marine transportation
  security in consultation with the port security committee;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the requirements of sections 363 to 375,
  384 to 386 and 392, subsection 393(2) and section 394 are fulfilled;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} operate the port in accordance with the port security
  plan; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} clearly mark with signs each restricted area at the
  port.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-270, s. 7
- SOR/2014-162, s. 42
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-362-20071102.html "Link to previous version of section 362"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Responsibilities of the Port Security Officer]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710824 .Subheading}

**[[363]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-363 .sectionLabel}** The port security
officer shall develop a port security plan with the marine facility
security officers at the port and in consultation with representatives
of federal departments and agencies, provincial and municipal
governments, appropriate law enforcement agencies, emergency response
providers, employers and labour at the port.

#### [Responsibilities of the Port Security Committee]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710826 .Subheading}

- **[[364]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-364 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  port security committee shall coordinate marine transportation
  security, which may include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the identification of critical infrastructure and
    operations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the identification of risks, threats,
    vulnerabilities and consequences;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the determination of mitigation strategies and
    implementation methods; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of a process to continually
    evaluate marine transportation security.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The port security committee shall meet at least once
  a year for the purpose of reviewing its responsibilities that result
  from the application of subsection (1) and determining on that basis
  whether any modifications are required to the port security plan.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 43
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-364-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 364"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Composition of the Port Security Committee]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710836 .Subheading}

**[[365]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-365 .sectionLabel}** The chair of the port
security committee may appoint other committee members from
organizations concerned or affected by the security of the port
including representatives of marine facilities, labour and municipal and
provincial governments.

#### [Port Security Assessments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710838 .Subheading}

**[[366]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-366 .sectionLabel}** Security assessment
information in respect of a port shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in English or French;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} be based on background information, the completion of
  an on-site survey and an analysis of that information and survey;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} identify and evaluate

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the physical aspects of the port that are the most
    important to protect and the means for protecting the personnel,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} possible threats to the port, and the likelihood
    of their occurrence, in order to establish security procedures and
    countermeasures and their order of priority, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the vulnerabilities, including human factors, in
    the security of the port;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} be protected from unauthorized access or disclosure;
  and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if stored in electronic format, have procedures to
  prevent its unauthorized deletion, destruction or amendment.

#### [Requirements for Persons Providing Port Security Assessment Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710848 .Subheading}

**[[367]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-367 .sectionLabel}** The persons who provide
port security assessment information shall have, collectively, the
competence to evaluate the security of the port, including knowledge in
the following areas:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} current security threats and patterns;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the detection and recognition of weapons, explosives
  and incendiaries and other dangerous substances and devices;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the recognition of the characteristics and
  behavioural patterns of persons who are likely to threaten security;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} techniques that might be used to violate security
  procedures or to circumvent security procedures, equipment or systems;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} methods used to cause a security incident;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the effects of dangerous substances and devices on
  structures and essential services;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} port security, marine facility and vessel security
  requirements;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} marine facility and vessel interface business
  practices;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} emergency preparedness and response and contingency
  planning;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} physical security requirements;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} radio and telecommunications systems, including
  computer systems and networks;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} marine or civil engineering; and

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} marine facility and vessel operations.

#### [Port Security Assessment Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710863 .Subheading}

**[[368]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-368 .sectionLabel}** Port security
assessment information shall consist of the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the general layout of the port, including the
  location of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} active and inactive access points to the port,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} security doors, barriers, and lighting,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} restricted areas,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} emergency and stand-by equipment available to
    maintain essential services,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} storage areas for maintenance equipment,
    unaccompanied baggage, ships' stores and cargo,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} escape and evacuation routes and assembly
    stations, and

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} existing security and safety equipment for the
    protection of personnel and visitors;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} changes in the tide that might have an impact on the
  vulnerability or security of the port;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a list of the emergency and stand-by equipment
  available to maintain essential services;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} for each marine facility in the port, the number of
  personnel and the security tasks of persons with security
  responsibilities;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} existing security and safety equipment for the
  protection of personnel and visitors at the port;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} escape and evacuation routes and assembly stations
  that have to be maintained to ensure the orderly and safe emergency
  evacuation of the port;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the results of security audits; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} security procedures in effect, including inspection
  and access control procedures, identification systems, surveillance
  and monitoring equipment, personnel identification documents and
  communication, alarm, lighting and other appropriate systems.

#### [Elements of Port Security Assessments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710880 .Subheading}

**[[369]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-369 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
conduct a port security assessment that addresses the following elements
in respect of the port, as applicable:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the physical security;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the structural integrity;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} personnel protection systems;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} operational procedures that might impact on security;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the radio and telecommunications systems, including
  computer systems and networks;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the relevant transportation support infrastructure;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} utilities;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} response organizations; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} other elements that might, if damaged or used
  illicitly, pose a risk to persons, property or operations at the port.

#### [On-site Survey and Vulnerability Assessments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710891 .Subheading}

**[[370]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-370 .sectionLabel}** The on-site survey
shall examine and evaluate current protective procedures and operations
to verify or collect port security assessment information.

- **[[371]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-371 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  port security assessment shall include a vulnerability assessment
  undertaken in consultation with the operators of marine facilities in
  the port and representatives of labour to determine the following so
  as to produce an overall assessment of the level of risk against which
  security procedures have to be developed:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any particular aspect of the port, including vessel
    traffic in the vicinity, that could make it a target of an attack;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the possible consequences of an attack against the
    port in terms of loss of life, damage to property and economic
    disruption, including disruption to marine transport systems;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the capability and intent of persons likely to
    mount an attack; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the possible types of attack.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The vulnerability assessment shall include a
  consideration of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} current security procedures, including
    identification systems;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} methods and points of access to the port;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to protect radio and
    telecommunications equipment, including computer systems and
    networks;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any conflicting policies between safety and
    security procedures;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any enforcement or personnel constraints;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} methods for monitoring restricted areas and other
    areas that have restricted access to ensure that only authorized
    persons have access;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} areas adjacent to the port that might be exploited
    during or for an attack;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} current security procedures relating to utilities
    and other services;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any deficiencies identified during training or
    drills;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} any deficiencies identified during daily operations
    or following incidents or alerts, reports of security concerns, the
    application of control measures or audits; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the structural integrity of the port.

### [Port Security Plan]{.HTitleText2} {#h-710911 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710912 .Subheading}

**[[372]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-372 .sectionLabel}** A port security plan

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be based on the findings of the port security
  assessment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall be in English or French;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall be protected from unauthorized access or
  disclosure;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} shall, if stored in electronic format, have
  procedures to prevent its unauthorized deletion, destruction or
  amendment; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} shall be submitted to the Minister for approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-162, s. 44
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-372-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 372"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Submission and Approval]{.HTitleText3} {#h-710921 .Subheading}

- **[[372.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-372.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is satisfied that a
  security plan meets the requirements of this Part, the Minister shall
  approve the security plan and issue a letter of approval certifying
  that the plan meets the requirements of this Part, unless approving
  the plan is not in the public interest and is likely to adversely
  affect marine transportation security.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The plan is valid for the period fixed by the
  Minister, which shall not exceed five years after the day on which it
  is approved by the Minister. In fixing the period, the Minister shall
  consider the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the port's operations and the industry in which it
    operates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the port administration's security record; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the complexity of the port security plan and the
    details of its procedures.
